CONSENT CALENDAR
All matters listed under the Consent Calendar are considered by the Council to be routine and will be enacted by one motion without
discussion. If discussion is desired, that item may be removed and considered separately.
 
4.
Citywide Comprehensive Speed Limit Update — Ordinance Adoption
Adopt an Ordinance establishing updated speed limits on 108 roadway segments citywide that do not result in an
increase to the existing posted speed limit, superseding applicable existing Ordinances establishing speed limits
on City roadways. School zone speed limits previously adopted will remain unaffected.
CEQA: This Ordinance adoption is categorically exempt from CEQA as an “existing facility,” because it consists of
the operation, repair, maintenance, permitting, leasing, licensing, or minor alteration of existing public or private
structures, facilities, mechanical equipment, or topographical features, involving negligible or no expansion of
use. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15301.)
5.
Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees Appointment Subcommittee — Appointment
Recommendation
1 of 207

--- page 2 ---
Appoint Mina Yaroslavsky Bressler to the Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees for a 4-year term
through October 7, 2030, and place Christina Galletti on a six-month eligibility list should a vacancy occur.
CEQA: This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it is an organizational or administrative activity that
will not result in direct or indirect physical changes in the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15378(b)(5).)
6.
WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant — Amendment
Approve Amendment No. 1 to the WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant Program Agreement to extend the
term to December 31, 2028; and authorize the City Manager to execute Amendment No. 1 in substantially the
form presented.
CEQA: Approval of Amendment No. 1 to the Westlight Energy Member Agency Grant Program Agreement is
exempt from CEQA as “existing facilities,” because the projects affected consist of the operation, repair,
maintenance, permitting, leasing, licensing, or minor alteration of existing public or private structures, facilities,
mechanical equipment, or topographical features, involving negligible or no expansion of existing or former use.
(CEQA Guidelines Section 15301.) In addition, the funding for electric equipment and vehicles is exempt under the
“common sense” exemption, in that it can be seen with certainty that there is no possibility that these activities in
question may have a significant effect on the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15061(b)(3).)
7.
Library Materials Acquisition – Agreement
Approve an Agreement with Midwest Tape LLC to provide physical and digital library materials including access to
the Hoopla Platform, which allows the library to provide their users with access to digital media content using
smart phones, tablets, computers, streaming devices, and web browsers in an amount not to exceed $300,000
annually for fiscal years 2025-26, 2026-27, and 2027-28, with an optional three-year term extension, for a
potential total agreement amount not to exceed $1,800,000; and authorize the City Manager to approve any
appropriate changes that do not increase the total not to exceed amount and execute the agreement on behalf
of the City.
CEQA: This agreement with Midwest Tape is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty
that it will not cause a physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)
8.
Collection System Construction Management Services for Various Projects — Agreements
Approve five-year agreements with three professional services firms, Tanner Pacific, Inc., 4Leaf, Inc., and Marina
Construction Management, Inc., in amounts not to exceed $3,000,000 each, to provide as-needed construction
management services for various sanitary sewer collection system projects and authorize the Public Works
Director to execute the agreements in substantially the form presented and issue project-specific task orders
under these agreements.
CEQA: Council action on this item is exempt from CEQA review under section 15061 (b)(3) of the CEQA Guidelines,
since entering into a professional services contract will not have a significant effect on the environment. The
resulting sewer collection system projects are Categorically Exempt from CEQA requirements under section 15301
of CEQA guidelines, as they will be replacing existing facilities.
9.
Child Care Facility Fund Program Loan Documents – Revision and Amendment
Approve a Loan Agreement Amendment, Deed of Trust, and Promissory Note with Mighty Tiger Preschool, LLC to
change the real property loan collateral for the Child Care Facilities Fund loan in the remaining amount of
$344,000; and authorize City Manager to execute the Loan Agreement Amendment, Deed of Trust, and
Promissory Note in substantially the form presented.

CEQA: This loan collateral change is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it
will not cause a physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)

Parking Enforcement Services — Amendment
Approve Amendment No. 4 to the agreement with Laz Parking for Citywide parking enforcement in the amount
of $1,581,576 for a new agreement total not to exceed $9,375,151; and authorize the Chief of Police to execute
the amendment in substantially the form presented.
CEQA: This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it will not cause a
physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)
11.
Grand Jury Report: "Is Our Flock of ALPR Cameras Properly Tended?" — Response Letter
Approve a letter responding to the August 3, 2026, San Mateo County Civil Grand Jury report titled "Is Our Flock
of ALPR Cameras Properly Tended?" and authorize the Mayor to send the response of behalf of the City Council.
CEQA: This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it is an organizational or administrative activity that
will not result in direct or indirect physical changes in the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15378(b)(5).)
12.
Master Services Purchasing Agreement — Amendment
Approve Amendment No. 1 to the Master Services and Purchasing Agreement with Axon Enterprise, Inc., to
provide Automated License Plate Readers and related technology for a 98 month term in the amount of
$1,145,260.45, for a new cumulative agreement total of $8,495,312.58; and authorize the Chief of Police to
execute the agreement in substantially the form presented.
CEQA: This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it will not cause a
physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)

Members of the public wishing to comment on any item not appearing on the agenda may address the City Council at this time. State
law prevents Council from taking action on any matter not on the agenda; your comments may be referred to staff for follow up.
Public comment is limited to a total of 15 minutes; however, an opportunity for additional public comment may be provided later in
the agenda.

1700 S. El Camino Real – Below Market Rate Alternative Plan – Approval
Adopt a Resolution to approve an alternative means of compliance with the City’s Below Market Rate Housing
Program pursuant to Section VIII(d) of the Below Market Rate Guidelines for the proposed 441-unit housing
development project located at 1700 S. El Camino Real (PA-2026-0025).
CEQA: The approval of an alternative means of compliance with the City’s Below Market Rate Housing Program is
not a project subject to CEQA, because it is an organizational or administrative activity that will not result in
direct or indirect physical changes in the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15378(b)(5).) The underlying
Project will undergo its own environmental review under CEQA as part of the development review process.

14.
Storm Drain Master Plan Update — Adoption
Approve a Resolution to adopt the updated Storm Drain Master Plan.
3 of 207

--- page 4 ---
CEQA: Adoption of the 2026 Storm Drain Master Plan is exempt from the California Environmental Quality Act
(CEQA) since it is a feasibility or planning study for possible future actions which the City of San Mateo has not
approved, adopted, or funded. The individual projects in the Plan are not yet funded, and each project will require
a separate CEQA analysis at time of design and construction. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15262.) Very high priority
and other individual capital improvement projects identified in the SDMP will be required to comply with CEQA
prior to implementation. The City will satisfy applicable CEQA requirements through the preparation of the
appropriate environmental documentation, such as an Environmental Impact Report (EIR) or Mitigated Negative
Declaration (MND), unless the project qualifies for a categorical exemption under CEQA.

PUBLIC COMMENTS/REQUEST TO SPEAK
Prior to the Meeting 
Send comments to: clerk@cityofsanmateo.org until 4 p.m. the day of the meeting.
During the meeting
By Zoom: Click the link at the top of the agenda and you’ll be added to the meeting. All attendees are muted by default. When the item of interest
is open for consideration, select the “Raise Your Hand” icon and you will be called on at the appropriate time.
By telephone: Call (408) 638-0968 and enter the conference ID found at the top of the meeting agenda. When the item of interest is open for
consideration, select *9 to raise your hand. When called upon, press *6 to unmute, state your name and provide your comments.
In Person: At the meeting complete a “Request to Speak” form. Organized Group and Presentations: Up to five (5) minutes for a group presentation,
with as many speakers as can be accommodated within that time allowance. To qualify for a group presentation, all individuals associated with the
presentation must be clearly identified and confirmed as in attendance at the meeting. Presentation materials must be submitted to
clerk@cityofsanmateo.org one (1) business day in advance of the meeting.
Presentations: PowerPoints, videos, or other media to be presented during public comment are accepted only by email to
4 of 207

--- page 5 ---
clerk@cityofsanmateo.org at least 24 hours prior to the meeting. Once received, the Clerk will have them shared at public comment for the
specified item. To uphold strong cybersecurity management practices, USB’s or other physical electronic storage devices are not accepted.
Signs: Signs and symbolic materials less than 2 feet by 3 feet are permitted provided that: (1) sticks, posts, poles or similar/other type of handle
objects are strictly prohibited; (2) the items do not create a facility, fire, or safety hazard; and (3) persons with such items remain seated when
displaying them and must not raise the items above shoulder level, obstruct the view or passage of other attendees, or otherwise disturb the
business of the meeting.
ACCESSIBILITY:  In compliance with the Americans with Disabilities Act and to ensure meaningful access for all participants, including those with
limited English proficiency, individuals requiring special accommodation or language assistance to participate in this meeting may contact the City
Clerk’s Office at (650) 522-7040 or clerk@cityofsanmateo.org. Notification 48 hours prior to the meeting will enable the City to make reasonable
arrangements to ensure accessibility to this meeting.
SPANISH INTERPRETATION: If you need interpretation of your communications to the City Council from Spanish to English or English to Spanish,
please contact the City Clerk Department at (650) 522-7040 or by email at clerk@cityofsanmateo.org. Notification of at least 48 hours will usually
enable the City to make arrangements.
INTERPRETACIÓN AL ESPAÑOL: Si necesita interpretación del inglés al español o del español al inglés para sus comunicaciones al Concejo de la
Ciudad, comuníquese con el Departmento del Secretario Municipal al (650) 522-7040 o por correo electrónico a clerk@cityofsanmateo.org. Por
favor, avísenos con al menos 48 horas de anticipación para que la ciudad pueda hacer los arreglos necesarios.

MEETING DATE:
October 05, 2026 
SUBJECT:
Citywide Comprehensive Speed Limit Update — Ordinance Adoption
RECOMMENDATION:
Adopt an Ordinance establishing updated speed limits on 108 roadway segments citywide that do not result in an increase 
to the existing posted speed limit, superseding applicable existing Ordinances establishing speed limits on City roadways. 
School zone speed limits previously adopted will remain unaffected.
BACKGROUND:
On September 22, 2026, the City Council considered the Citywide Comprehensive Speed Limit Update and introduced a 
modified Ordinance establishing updated speed limits on roadway segments evaluated through the Engineering and Traffic 
Survey (ETS) process.
Following discussion, the City Council introduced a modified Ordinance that includes the 108 roadway segments where the 
recommended speed limit would either remain unchanged or decrease from the existing posted speed limit. The City 
Council directed staff to exclude the remaining 15 roadway segments where the ETS recommended a speed limit increase 
and to return those segments for further discussion at a future study session.
The changes requested by the City Council following introduction are reflected in the attached Ordinance, including both 
the clean version provided as Attachment 1 and the tracked changes version provided as Attachment 2.
If adopted, the Ordinance will become effective 30 days after adoption. 
BUDGET IMPACT:
Implementation of the recommended speed limit changes will require sign verification and updates across the affected 
roadway segments. This work will be completed by a combination of contracted work and work performed by the Public 
Works Department’s Field Maintenance Division. The total cost is estimated at approximately $50,000. Costs will be 
absorbed within the existing Citywide Engineering and Traffic Study Update (46R034) project budget and are not expected 
to require additional appropriation.
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This Ordinance adoption is categorically exempt from CEQA as an “existing facility,” because it consists of the operation, 
repair, maintenance, permitting, leasing, licensing, or minor alteration of existing public or private structures, facilities, 
mechanical equipment, or topographical features, involving negligible or no expansion of use. (CEQA Guidelines Section 
15301.)

CITY OF SAN MATEO
DRAFT ORDINANCE
An Ordinance of the City of San Mateo Establishing Updated Prima Facie Speed Limits on Certain City Streets 
Based on Engineering and Traffic Surveys, and Superseding Prior Ordinances Establishing Speed Limits on City 
Streets
WHEREAS, the City of San Mateo's 2026 Strategic Plan identifies safety as part of the Transportation 
Priority Initiative, and in furtherance of that Initiative, the City's Public Works and Police Departments have 
advanced programs, enforcement efforts, and infrastructure improvements intended to reduce collisions, 
encourage safer travel behavior, and support a transportation network that serves all users; and
WHEREAS, speed management remains a core tool for improving roadway safety by encouraging 
appropriate vehicle speeds, improving compliance with posted speed limits, and reducing the potential for 
serious injury or death when collisions occur, consistent with the City's General Plan 2040 Vision Zero policy and 
the City's Local Roadway Safety Plan (LRSP), both of which are grounded in the Safe Systems approach; and
WHEREAS, beginning in 2022 the City prioritized speed management around schools and, by resolution 
of the City Council, established 15 mph school zone speed limits and related 25 mph school zone approach 
speed limits citywide, with installation of associated signage completed citywide in early 2024, and such school 
zone speed limits remain in full force and effect; and
WHEREAS, California Vehicle Code Section 22358 and related provisions of the Vehicle Code authorize 
local authorities, on the basis of an engineering and traffic survey, to declare prima facie speed limits on City 
streets that are not otherwise subject to a speed limit established by state law; and
WHEREAS, the City evaluated all roadways citywide to determine which street segments require an 
engineering and traffic survey (ETS) under state law in order to remain enforceable using radar, lidar, or other 
speed-measuring devices, which identified 108 roadway segments requiring an ETS, and that all other remaining 
City streets would be subject to the applicable default speed limits established under state law; and
WHEREAS, the City conducted engineering and traffic surveys for said 108 roadway segments, applying 
the flexibility afforded under state law to establish the lowest defensible speed limit appropriate to each 
roadway's context, function, and safety needs, while preserving the City's ability to use radar, lidar, and other 
appropriate enforcement tools; and
WHEREAS, the results of said engineering and traffic surveys, and the resulting prima facie speed limits 
recommended for the 108 evaluated roadway segments, are set forth in Exhibit A to this Ordinance, attached 
hereto and incorporated herein by reference; and
WHEREAS, state law requires that speed limits established through the engineering and traffic survey 
process be adopted by local ordinance and speed limit signs installed before they may be enforced; and
WHEREAS, the City currently has multiple, older ordinances establishing prima facie speed limits on 
individual City streets, and the City Council desires to supersede those prior ordinances and consolidate the
8 of 207

--- page 9 ---
applicable speed limits into fewer ordinances in order to streamline administration, reduce fragmentation 
across multiple prior ordinances, and simplify future updates; and
WHEREAS, the engineering and traffic survey results and speed limits set forth in Exhibit A are valid for 
seven years from the date of the underlying survey, and such validity may be extended to fourteen years where 
a registered engineer determines that no significant changes in roadway or traffic conditions have occurred, 
consistent with California Vehicle Code section 40802; and
WHEREAS, the school zone speed limits described above were adopted by resolution rather than by 
ordinance, are not among the ordinances being superseded by this ordinance, and are not otherwise affected by 
this ordinance; and
WHEREAS, the prima facie speed limits established by this ordinance shall become effective only upon 
passage of this ordinance and the posting of appropriate signs giving notice thereof on the respective street 
segments.
NOW, THEREFORE, THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF SAN MATEO ORDAINS AS FOLLOWS:
Section 1. Findings. The recitals set forth above are true and correct and are incorporated herein by this 
reference as findings of the City Council.
Section 2. Pursuant to Section 22358 of the Vehicle Code of the State of California, the City Council has 
determined, on the basis of engineering and traffic surveys, that the speed limits set forth in Attachment 
1 (Schedule of Prima Facie Speed Limits), attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference, will 
facilitate the orderly movement of vehicular traffic, are reasonable and safe, and are hereby declared to 
be the prima facie speed limits for the respective street segments identified therein. Said prima facie 
speed limits shall be effective when appropriate signs giving notice thereof are erected upon the 
respective street segments.
Section 3. Prior Ordinances Superseded. Except as provided in Section Four below, and except as 
provided in a companion ordinance establishing prima facie speed limits on two South Delaware Street 
segments, all ordinances, and all provisions of ordinances, of the City of San Mateo establishing prima 
facie speed limits on City streets are hereby superseded in their entirety by this Ordinance, as specified 
in Exhibit A, attached hereto and incorporated by reference.
Section 4. School Zone Speed Limits Unaffected. Nothing in this Ordinance shall repeal, amend, or 
otherwise affect the 15 mph school zone speed limits, or the related 25 mph school zone approach 
speed limits, previously established by Resolution No. 120-2021 of the City Council.
Section 5. Environmental Determination. This action is categorically exempt as an “existing facility” 
pursuant to CEQA Guidelines Section 15301, because it involves minor alterations of existing city streets 
and involves negligible or no expansion of an existing or former use.
Section 6. Severability. In the event any section, clause or provision of this ordinance shall be 
determined invalid or unconstitutional, such section, clause or provision shall be deemed severable and 
all other sections or portions hereof shall remain in full force and effect.
9 of 207

--- page 10 ---
Section 7. Publication. This ordinance shall be published in summary in a newspaper of general 
circulation, posted in the City Clerk's Office, and posted on the City's website, all in accord with Section
2.15 of the City Charter.
Section 8. Legislative History and Effective Date. This ordinance was introduced on Clerk to complete., 
and adopted on Clerk to complete., and shall be effective 30 days after its adoption.
10 of 207

CITY OF SAN MATEO
DRAFT ORDINANCE
An Ordinance of the City of San Mateo Establishing Updated Prima Facie Speed Limits on Certain City Streets 
Based on Engineering and Traffic Surveys, and Superseding Prior Ordinances Establishing Speed Limits on City 
Streets
WHEREAS, the City of San Mateo's 2026 Strategic Plan identifies safety as part of the Transportation 
Priority Initiative, and in furtherance of that Initiative, the City's Public Works and Police Departments have 
advanced programs, enforcement efforts, and infrastructure improvements intended to reduce collisions, 
encourage safer travel behavior, and support a transportation network that serves all users; and
WHEREAS, speed management remains a core tool for improving roadway safety by encouraging 
appropriate vehicle speeds, improving compliance with posted speed limits, and reducing the potential for 
serious injury or death when collisions occur, consistent with the City's General Plan 2040 Vision Zero policy and 
the City's Local Roadway Safety Plan (LRSP), both of which are grounded in the Safe Systems approach; and
WHEREAS, beginning in 2022 the City prioritized speed management around schools and, by resolution 
of the City Council, established 15 mph school zone speed limits and related 25 mph school zone approach 
speed limits citywide, with installation of associated signage completed citywide in early 2024, and such school 
zone speed limits remain in full force and effect; and
WHEREAS, California Vehicle Code Section 22358 and related provisions of the Vehicle Code authorize 
local authorities, on the basis of an engineering and traffic survey, to declare prima facie speed limits on City 
streets that are not otherwise subject to a speed limit established by state law; and
WHEREAS, the City evaluated all roadways citywide to determine which street segments require an 
engineering and traffic survey (ETS) under state law in order to remain enforceable using radar, lidar, or other 
speed-measuring devices, which identified 121108 roadway segments requiring an ETS, and that all other 
remaining City streets would be subject to the applicable default speed limits established under state law; and
WHEREAS, the City conducted engineering and traffic surveys for said 121108 roadway segments, 
applying the flexibility afforded under state law to establish the lowest defensible speed limit appropriate to 
each roadway's context, function, and safety needs, while preserving the City's ability to use radar, lidar, and 
other appropriate enforcement tools; and
WHEREAS, the results of said engineering and traffic surveys, and the resulting prima facie speed limits 
recommended for the 121108 evaluated roadway segments, are set forth in Exhibit A to this Ordinance, 
attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference; and
WHEREAS, state law requires that speed limits established through the engineering and traffic survey 
process be adopted by local ordinance and speed limit signs installed before they may be enforced; and
WHEREAS, the City currently has multiple, older ordinances establishing prima facie speed limits on 
individual City streets, and the City Council desires to supersede those prior ordinances and consolidate the
19 of 207

--- page 20 ---
applicable speed limits into fewer ordinances in order to streamline administration, reduce fragmentation 
across multiple prior ordinances, and simplify future updates; and
WHEREAS, the engineering and traffic survey results and speed limits set forth in Exhibit A are valid for 
seven years from the date of the underlying survey, and such validity may be extended to fourteen years where 
a registered engineer determines that no significant changes in roadway or traffic conditions have occurred, 
consistent with California Vehicle Code section 40802; and
WHEREAS, the school zone speed limits described above were adopted by resolution rather than by 
ordinance, are not among the ordinances being superseded by this ordinance, and are not otherwise affected by 
this ordinance; and
WHEREAS, the prima facie speed limits established by this ordinance shall become effective only upon 
passage of this ordinance and the posting of appropriate signs giving notice thereof on the respective street 
segments.
NOW, THEREFORE, THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF SAN MATEO ORDAINS AS FOLLOWS:
Section 1. Findings. The recitals set forth above are true and correct and are incorporated herein by this 
reference as findings of the City Council.
Section 2. Pursuant to Section 22358 of the Vehicle Code of the State of California, the City Council has 
determined, on the basis of engineering and traffic surveys, that the speed limits set forth in Attachment 
1 (Schedule of Prima Facie Speed Limits), attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference, will 
facilitate the orderly movement of vehicular traffic, are reasonable and safe, and are hereby declared to 
be the prima facie speed limits for the respective street segments identified therein. Said prima facie 
speed limits shall be effective when appropriate signs giving notice thereof are erected upon the 
respective street segments.
Section 3. Prior Ordinances Superseded. Except as provided in Section Four below, and except as 
provided in a companion ordinance establishing prima facie speed limits on two South Delaware Street 
segments, all ordinances, and all provisions of ordinances, of the City of San Mateo establishing prima 
facie speed limits on City streets are hereby superseded in their entirety by this Ordinance, as specified 
in Exhibit A, attached hereto and incorporated by reference.
Section 4. School Zone Speed Limits Unaffected. Nothing in this Ordinance shall repeal, amend, or 
otherwise affect the 15 mph school zone speed limits, or the related 25 mph school zone approach 
speed limits, previously established by Resolution No. 120-2021 of the City Council.
Section 5. Environmental Determination. This action is categorically exempt as an “existing facility” 
pursuant to CEQA Guidelines Section 15301, because it involves minor alterations of existing city streets 
and involves negligible or no expansion of an existing or former use.
Section 6. Severability. In the event any section, clause or provision of this ordinance shall be 
determined invalid or unconstitutional, such section, clause or provision shall be deemed severable and 
all other sections or portions hereof shall remain in full force and effect.
20 of 207

--- page 21 ---
Section 7. Publication. This ordinance shall be published in summary in a newspaper of general 
circulation, posted in the City Clerk's Office, and posted on the City's website, all in accord with Section
2.15 of the City Charter.
Section 8. Legislative History and Effective Date. This ordinance was introduced on Clerk to complete., 
and adopted on Clerk to complete., and shall be effective 30 days after its adoption.

SUBJECT:
Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees Appointment Subcommittee — Appointment Recommendation
RECOMMENDATION:
Appoint Mina Yaroslavsky Bressler to the Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees for a 4-year term through 
October 7, 2030, and place Christina Galletti on a six-month eligibility list should a vacancy occur.
BACKGROUND:
In November 2023, Ed Degliantoni, the City’s appointee to the San Mateo County Mosquito & Vector Control District Board 
of Trustees, formally submitted his resignation. Following his departure, the City Clerk’s Office conducted a recruitment to 
fill the vacancy. In September 2024, following an interview and recommendation by the Appointment Subcommittee, the 
City Council appointed Tolifili Fa to serve a two-year term through October 7, 2026.
With the expiration of the current term approaching, the City Clerk’s Office conducted a recruitment for the next term on 
the Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees. The City received four applications from Scott Michael Lee, 
Christina Galletti, Mina Yaroslavky Bressier, and incumbent Tolifili Fa.
Three of the four applicants moved forward in the process and were interviewed by the Appointment Subcommittee. 
Tolifili Fa withdrew her application from consideration. Following the interviews, the Appointment Subcommittee 
considered the applicants’ qualifications, experience, and interest in serving as the City of San Mateo’s representative on 
the Mosquito & Vector Control District Board of Trustees.
The Appointment Subcommittee recommends the following actions:
Recommended Action
Duration
Mina Yaroslavsky Bressler be appointed to a 4-year term
October 7, 2026 through October 7, 2030
Christina Galletti
Immediate through April 5, 2027
BUDGET IMPACT:
There is no budgetary impact associated with this administrative action. 
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it is an organizational or administrative activity that will not result in 
direct or indirect physical changes in the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15378(b)(5).)
STRATEGIC PLAN ALIGNMENT
This staff report supports the Organizational Health and Good Governance initiative.

SUBJECT:
WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant — Amendment
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve Amendment No. 1 to the WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant Program Agreement to extend the term to 
December 31, 2028; and authorize the City Manager to execute Amendment No. 1 in substantially the form presented.
BACKGROUND:
WestLight Energy (formerly Peninsula Clean Energy) is a joint powers authority developed in 2016 to reduce greenhouse 
gas emissions by providing electricity from clean energy sources. In 2024, the WestLight Energy Board of Directors 
authorized a one-time allocation of $11.5 million in surplus funds for member agencies to implement local energy projects 
through the WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant Program. This is a noncompetitive funding source for which the City 
of San Mateo is eligible for $1,406,718. Eligible projects must align with WestLight Energy’s goal of carbon neutrality 
through the advancement of electrification of transportation and buildings.
On December 9, 2024, the City Council approved an agreement with WestLight Energy and adopted a resolution to accept 
and appropriate the WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant Program funding to support the following projects:
1.
Electric vehicle (EV) charging infrastructure for City Fleet
2.
Electrifying the Marina Library
3.
High Voltage Streetlight Conversion
4.
Electric Landscaping Equipment for City Operations
5.
Pilot Electric Motorcycle for Police Department
The current WestLight Energy Member Agency Grant agreement expires on December 31, 2026. The City has made 
progress on the implementation of all five projects but needs additional time to complete the EV charging infrastructure 
for City fleet and the electrification of Marina Library. The proposed amendment does not provide or request any 
additional funding; it solely extends the term of the existing agreement through December 31, 2028 to allow the City 
additional time to complete the remaining projects using previously awarded grant funds. 
BUDGET IMPACT:
This Agreement Amendment No. 1 has no additional budget impact, as it only extends the term of the existing agreement 
and does not provide or require additional funding. A previous City Council action on December 9, 2024 adopted a 
resolution to accept and appropriate the $1,406,718 grant funding into various Capital Improvement Program projects 
including Green Fleet Charging Infrastructure Project 46C003, the Marina Library Structural Improvement Project 665100, 
and the High Voltage Streetlight Conversion Project 460040.
34 of 207

--- page 35 ---
CITY OF SAN MATEO
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
Approval of Amendment No. 1 to the Westlight Energy Member Agency Grant Program Agreement is exempt from CEQA 
as “existing facilities,” because the projects affected consist of the operation, repair, maintenance, permitting, leasing, 
licensing, or minor alteration of existing public or private structures, facilities, mechanical equipment, or topographical 
features, involving negligible or no expansion of existing or former use. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15301.)  In addition, the 
funding for electric equipment and vehicles is exempt under the “common sense” exemption, in that it can be seen with 
certainty that there is no possibility that these activities in question may have a significant effect on the environment. 
(CEQA Guidelines Section 15061(b)(3).)
STRATEGIC PLAN ALIGNMENT
This staff report supports the Climate Resiliency initiative.
NOTICE PROVIDED
All meeting noticing requirements were met.

FROM:

PREPARED BY:
Library Department
MEETING DATE:
October 5, 2026 
SUBJECT:
Library Materials Acquisition – Agreement
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve an Agreement with Midwest Tape LLC to provide physical and digital library materials including access to the 
Hoopla Platform, which allows the library to provide their users with access to digital media content using smart phones, 
tablets, computers, streaming devices, and web browsers in an amount not to exceed $300,000 annually for fiscal years 
2025-26, 2026-27, and 2027-28, with an optional three-year term extension, for a potential total agreement amount not to 
exceed $1,800,000; and authorize the City Manager to approve any appropriate changes that do not increase the total not 
to exceed amount and execute the agreement on behalf of the City.
BACKGROUND:
In October 2025, the Library's primary distributor of physical books and media, Baker & Taylor, abruptly ceased operations 
without prior notice. Library staff immediately initiated a replacement search, evaluating potential vendors across key 
criteria, including customer support, inventory range, collection development, cataloging services, pricing, and fulfilment 
rates. Following evaluation, staff selected Ingram Services LLC as the primary physical materials provider. However, items 
unavailable through Ingram must now be sourced from Midwest Tape, the Library's long standing digital provider of the 
Hoopla Platform. Expanding Midwest Tape's scope to include physical items increases total spending with the vendor, 
crossing the threshold that requires City Council approval. 
BUDGET IMPACT:
The costs associated with the agreement were included in the FY2025-26 and FY2026-27 approved budgets. Costs for 
subsequent years will be incorporated into future departmental budget appropriations.
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This agreement with Midwest Tape is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it will not 
cause a physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)
STRATEGIC PLAN ALIGNMENT
This staff report supports the Organizational Health and Good Governance initiative.
NOTICE PROVIDED
All meeting noticing requirements were met.

Att 1 – Proposed Agreement

AGREEMENT WITH MIDWEST TAPE, LLC
FOR DIGITAL AND PHYSICAL MEDIA SERVICES
This Agreement, made and entered into this day of September 23, 2026, by and between the

RECITALS:
A.

access to CONTRACTOR’S Hoopla Platform, which allows participating libraries to provide their users 
with access to digital media content using smart phones, tablets, computers, streaming devices, and 
web browsers, as hereinafter described.
B.
CITY desires to engage CONTRACTOR to provide these Digital and Physical Media 
Services by reason of its qualifications and experience for performing such services and CONTRACTOR 
has offered to provide the required services on the terms and in the manner set forth herein.
NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS AGREED as follows:

.

Digital Media Services under this Agreement is as described in Exhibit A to this Agreement, which is 
attached and incorporated by reference. As to Physical Media Services, CONTRACTOR will fulfill orders 
for physical media as placed by the CITY and in accordance with CONTRACTOR's standard terms for the 
sale of physical media.
SECTION 2 - DUTIES OF CONTRACTOR

Agreement.

the services of CONTRACTOR.

Municipal Code Chapter 5.92, which differs from the state minimum wage and includes a Consumer 
Price Index escalator.

prior to such termination.

SECTION 7 – OWNERSHIP; INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY
Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, CITY shall have no right, title, or 
interest in or to CONTRACTOR’s Hoopla Platform, any digital titles or other content made available 
through the Hoopla Platform (collectively, “Digital Titles”), the Hoopla applications, the Hoopla 
website, the Library Administration Website, or any of CONTRACTOR’s trademarks, data, artwork, or 
other materials delivered by or on behalf of CONTRACTOR in connection with this Agreement. CITY 
may not access, use, modify, or reproduce any portion of any source code relating to the Hoopla 
Platform, or make, sell, or distribute any variations or derivative works of the Hoopla Platform. This 
Agreement does not grant CITY, or the patrons of its library system, any public performance rights in 
the Digital Titles, and neither CITY nor its library patrons may offer any Digital Titles as a performance 
to the public, whether sponsored by CITY or otherwise. Vendors that supply Digital Titles and related 
materials to CONTRACTOR retain all of their intellectual property rights in and to such Digital Titles and 
materials. As between the parties, CONTRACTOR owns and retains all intellectual property rights in and 
to the Hoopla Platform, the Hoopla applications, the Hoopla website, the Library Administration 
Website, and all other of CONTRACTOR’s intellectual property, including but not limited to all 
modifications, updates, or improvements made thereto.
SECTION 8 - CONFIDENTIALITY
All reports, documents, data, and other materials prepared by CONTRACTOR in connection with 
the performance of this Agreement are confidential until released by CITY to the public or otherwise 
authorized for release by CITY, subject to applicable law. CONTRACTOR shall not make any such 
reports, documents, data, materials, or related non-public information available to any individual or 
organization not employed by CONTRACTOR or CITY without the prior written consent of CITY, except 
to CONTRACTOR’s subcontractors, agents, legal advisors, or professional advisors who need access for 
purposes of this Agreement and are informed of the confidential nature of the information, or as 
required by law.
To the extent CONTRACTOR provides CITY with non-public proprietary, technical, security, business, or 
trade secret information that CONTRACTOR identifies as confidential, CITY shall treat such information 
as confidential to the extent permitted by applicable law. CONTRACTOR acknowledges that CITY is a 
California public entity and that records in CITY’s possession may be subject to disclosure under the 
California Public Records Act or other applicable law. If CITY receives a request for records that CITY 
reasonably determines may include CONTRACTOR’s identified confidential information, CITY will make 
reasonable efforts, to the extent legally permitted and reasonably practicable, to notify CONTRACTOR 
before disclosure so that CONTRACTOR may seek appropriate protection.  
SECTION 9 – INTEREST OF CONTRACTOR
CONTRACTOR covenants that it presently has no interest, and shall not acquire any interest,

performance of the services under this Agreement.

mediation.  The parties shall meet in mediation within 30 days of a request.  The mediator shall be

name from mediators listed by either the American Arbitration Association, the State Mediation and 
Conciliation Service, or other agreed-upon service.  The mediator shall be selected by a blind draw.
The cost of mediation shall be borne equally by the parties.  Neither party shall be deemed the 
prevailing party.  No party shall be permitted to file a legal action without first meeting in mediation 
and making a good faith attempt to reach a mediated settlement.  The mediation process, once

EXHIBIT A
SCOPE OF SERVICES
Vendor will provide certain Digital and Physical Media Services, including, without limitation, access to 
the Hoopla Platform, which allows libraries to provide their users with access to digital media content 
using smart phones, tablets, computers, streaming devices, and web browsers.
As to Physical Media Services, Vendor will fulfill orders for physical media as placed by the City and in 
accordance with Vendor’s standard terms for the sale of physical media.
The chart below describes the distribution models included in Midwest Tape’s hoopla offering as of the 
Effective Date, including the digital media licenses that are available via the Digital Media Platform.
Instant Borrows
Flex Borrows
License type
PPU (Pay-Per Use)
OCOU (One-Copy/One-User)
Circulations /
Borrows
An   Instant   Title   is   a   work   that   is 
available for simultaneous access by 
multiple users. Instant Titles may be 
accessed  via  PPU  Circulations, 
otherwise referred to as Instant Borrows, 
each of which allows a user to stream, 
download, and/or access the chosen 
Instant Title, or collection of Titles (e.g., a 
“Binge Pass”, described below), for a 
limited time that terminates upon the 
expiration of a fixed period or earlier 
“return” by the user.   Instant Borrows are 
available regardless of whether another 
user has borrowed the same title for use 
at the same time. For each Instant 
Borrow, the Library purchases one Instant 
License to authorize that particular 
borrow.   A “Binge Pass” is an Instant 
Borrow that provides the borrower 
access to a collection of titles, either 
directly through hoopla or through a 
third-party website or application.
A Flex Title is only available to one user at a time, 
via a digital OCOU Circulation, otherwise 
referred to as a Flex Borrow. If the Library has an 
available Flex License to a Flex Title, a Patron 
may activate a Flex Borrow and that license is 
digitally checked out and considered “in use.” 
Other users cannot access that title during that 
borrow unless (i) the Library has purchased 
additional Flex Licenses to the same title, one of 
which is available at that time, or (ii) the same 
title also is available as an Instant Title. A Flex 
Borrow allows a user to stream, download, 
and/or access the title for a limited time that 
terminates upon the expiration of a fixed period 
or earlier “return” by the user.
Available 
Formats
All  formats  (audiobooks,  eBooks, 
comics, movies, television, music, and 
magazines).  Binge Passes are available 
only for select titles and may not be 
available for all formats.
Currently available only for audiobooks and 
eBooks.

Payment 
obligations
A  PPU-Circulation  Fee  is  charged  for
each Instant Borrow.   The fees, which 
are listed on the Library Administration 
Website, vary by title and format, and are 
subject to change from time to time.  
Where  available,  Flex  Licenses  may  be
ordered 
via 
the 
hoopla 
Digital 
Library 
Administration   Website.   The   costs   of these 
licenses vary by title and format. Fees for Flex 
Licenses are invoiced after each purchase 
(typically daily).

EXHIBIT C
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS
MINIMUM SCOPE OF INSURANCE 
Coverage shall be at least as broad as:
1. Commercial General Liability (CGL):  Insurance Services Office (ISO) Form CG 00 01 12 07 
covering CGL on an “occurrence” basis, including products-completed operations, personal & 
advertising injury, with limits no less than $2,000,000 per occurrence. If a general aggregate limit 
applies, either the general aggregate limit shall apply separately to this project/location or the 
general aggregate limit shall be twice the required occurrence limit.
2. Automobile Liability:  ISO Form Number CA 00 01 covering any auto (Code 1), or if Contractor 
has no owned autos, hired, (Code 8) and non-owned autos (Code 9), with limit no less than 
$1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury and property damage.
3. Workers’ Compensation: as required by the State of California, with Statutory Limits, and 
Employer’s Liability Insurance with limit of no less than $1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury 
or disease. 
If the contractor maintains higher limits than the minimums shown above, the City requires and shall be 
entitled to coverage for the higher limits maintained by the contractor.
Other Insurance Provisions
The insurance policies are to contain, or be endorsed to contain, the following provisions:
Additional Insured Status
The City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and agents are to be covered as insureds on 
the auto policy for liability arising out of automobiles owned, leased, hired or borrowed by or on behalf 
of the Contractor; and on the CGL policy with respect to liability arising out of work or operations 
performed by or on behalf of the Contractor including materials, parts or equipment furnished in 
connection with such work or operations. General liability coverage can be provided in the form of an 
endorsement to the Contractor’s insurance (at least as broad as ISO Form CG 20 10, 11 85 or both CG 
20 10 and CG 20 37 forms if later revisions used).
Primary Coverage
For any claims related to this contract, the Contractor’s insurance coverage shall be primary insurance 
as respects the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and agents. Any insurance or self-
insurance maintained by the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, or agents shall be 
excess of the Contractor’s insurance and shall not contribute with it.
Notice of Cancellation
Each insurance policy required above shall provide that coverage shall not be canceled, except after 
thirty (30) days’ prior written notice (10 days for non-payment) has been given to the City.

Waiver of Subrogation

subrogation, but this provision applies regardless of whether or not the City has received a waiver of

Deductibles and Self-Insured Retentions
Any deductibles or self-insured retentions must be declared to and approved by the City. The City may 
require the Contractor to purchase coverage with a lower deductible or retention or provide proof of 
ability to pay losses and related investigations, claim administration, and defense expenses within the 
retention.  
Acceptability of Insurers

otherwise acceptable to the City.
Verification of Coverage

the applicable policy language effecting coverage required by this clause.  All certificates and 
endorsements are to be received and approved by the City before work commences.  However, failure 
to obtain the required documents prior to the work beginning shall not waive the Contractor’s 
obligation to provide them.  The City reserves the right to require complete, certified copies of all 
required insurance policies, including endorsements required by these specifications, at any time.

SUBJECT:
Collection System Construction Management Services for Various Projects — Agreements
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve five-year agreements with three professional services firms, Tanner Pacific, Inc., 4Leaf, Inc., and Marina 
Construction Management, Inc., in amounts not to exceed $3,000,000 each, to provide as-needed construction 
management services for various sanitary sewer collection system projects and authorize the Public Works Director to 
execute the agreements in substantially the form presented and issue project-specific task orders under these 
agreements. 
BACKGROUND:
The sanitary sewer collection system requires regular rehabilitation and various infrastructure upgrades to improve 
wastewater system capacity, flow management, and reduce inflow and infiltration to comply with regulatory 
requirements. Construction management services will provide as-needed support for future collection system 
improvement projects, which include sewer rehabilitations/repair, lift station rehabilitations/upgrades, and increased 
sewer capacity improvements related to new developments. 
In May 2026, a Request for Qualifications (RFQ) for as-needed construction management services for post-Clean Water 
Program collection system projects was issued. Six firms responded to the RFQ. Staff evaluated the proposals based on 
proposal quality, key staff, project understanding, past performance, local experience, scope and approach, references, 
and skill set, and determined that three consultants were best qualified to provide the required services for the Clean 
Water Program: Tanner Pacific, Inc. (Tanner Pacific), 4Leaf, Inc. (4Leaf), and Marina Construction Management, Inc. 
(MCM). The three retained consultants may provide on-call construction management services for collection system 
evaluation and rehabilitation needs.
Staff recommends awarding agreements to Tanner Pacific, 4Leaf, and MCM, each in an amount not to exceed $3,000,000, 
for a five-year term through June 30, 2031. The scope of work for project-specific task orders will be determined on an as-
needed basis and issued by the Public Works Director.
BUDGET IMPACT:
There are sufficient funds in the Annual Sanitary Sewer Rehabilitation (46s191) and Sanitary Sewer CCTV Inspection 
Program (46s193) project budgets to fund these three agreements. 
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
Council action on this item is exempt from CEQA review under section 15061 (b)(3) of the CEQA Guidelines, since entering 
into a professional services contract will not have a significant effect on the environment. The resulting sewer collection 
system projects are Categorically Exempt from CEQA requirements under section 15301 of CEQA guidelines, as they will be 
51 of 207

CITY OF SAN MATEO
replacing existing facilities.

same or similar locality under similar circumstances, and CONSULTANT agrees to thus perform the 
work.  The acceptance of CONSULTANT's work by CITY does not operate as a release of CONSULTANT 
from said obligation. CONSULTANT shall not be responsible for errors or omissions to the extent 
resulting from inaccurate information, documents, or directives provided by CITY. 
SECTION 15 - WAIVERS
The waiver by either party of any breach or violation of any term, covenant, or condition of this 
Agreement or of any provisions of any ordinance or law shall not be deemed to be a waiver of such 
term, covenant, condition, ordinance or law or of any subsequent breach or violation of the same or of 
any other term, condition, ordinance, or law.  The subsequent acceptance by either party of any fee or 
other money which may become due hereunder shall not be deemed to be a waiver of any preceding 
breach or violation by the other party of any term, covenant, or condition of this Agreement or of any 
applicable law or ordinance.
SECTION 16 - SEVERABILITY
If any term or portion of this Agreement is held to be invalid, illegal, or otherwise 
unenforceable by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remaining provisions of this Agreement shall 
continue in full force and effect.
SECTION 17 - COSTS AND ATTORNEY FEES
Attorney fees in total amount not exceeding $5000, shall be recoverable as costs (by the filing 
of a cost bill) by the prevailing party in any action or actions to enforce the provisions of this 
Agreement.  The above $5000 limit is the total of attorney fees recoverable whether in the trial court, 
appellate court, or otherwise, and regardless of the number of attorneys, trials, appeals, or actions.  It 
is the intent of this Agreement that neither party shall have to pay the other more than $5000 for 
attorney fees arising out of an action, or actions to enforce the provisions of this Agreement.
SECTION 18 - NON-DISCRIMINATION
CONSULTANT warrants that it is an Equal Opportunity Employer and shall comply with 
applicable regulations governing equal employment opportunity.  Neither CONSULTANT nor any of its 
subcontractors shall discriminate in the employment of any person because of race, color, national 
origin, ancestry, physical handicap, medical condition, marital status, sex, or age, unless based upon a 
bona fide occupational qualification pursuant to the California Fair Employment and Housing Act.
56 of 207

--- page 57 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 5 of 21
Rev 4/26
SECTION 19 - MEDIATION
Should any dispute arise out of this Agreement, any party may request that it be submitted to 
mediation.  The parties shall meet in mediation within 30 days of a request.  The mediator shall be 
agreed to by the mediating parties; in the absence of an agreement, the parties shall each submit one 
name from mediators listed by either the American Arbitration Association, the State Mediation and 
Conciliation Service, or other agreed-upon service.  The mediator shall be selected by a blind draw.
The cost of mediation shall be borne equally by the parties.  Neither party shall be deemed the 
prevailing party.  No party shall be permitted to file a legal action without first meeting in mediation 
and making a good faith attempt to reach a mediated settlement.  The mediation process, once 
commenced by a meeting with the mediator, shall last until agreement is reached by the parties but 
not more than 60 days, unless the maximum time is extended by the parties.
SECTION 20 - LITIGATION
CONSULTANT shall testify at CITY'S request if litigation is brought against CITY in connection 
with CONSULTANT's services under this Agreement.  Unless the action is brought by CONSULTANT, or 
is based upon CONSULTANT's wrongdoing, CITY shall compensate CONSULTANT for preparation for 
testimony, testimony, and travel at CONSULTANT's standard hourly rates at the time of actual 
testimony.
SECTION 21 - NOTICES

Scope of Services
Task 1 – General Services
Project management and coordination include tasks that will be performed by the Consultant throughout the 
project. 
These services include:
General Project Management and Coordination
Risk Management
Quality Control
Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors, if applicable
Agency Coordination and City Meeting Support
Task 1.1 – Project Management 
Task 1.1.1 – General Management
The Consultant will be responsible for detailed management of the project, including subconsultants and 
subcontractors, and will keep the City/Construction Management Lead (CML) appraised of the status of the 
project.
The Consultant will provide the key management personnel as described in their proposal on this project. The 
Consultant will not reassign the key project personnel without prior approval of the City/CML per the Standard 
Agreement for Professional Consultant Services.
Task 1.1.2 – Progress Report
The Consultant will prepare and submit monthly progress reports that include a narrative of the work completed 
by task and project invoicing. The invoices will comply with Program standards and document the following:
•
The man-hours and billing rates for staff for each task in the WBS
•
All direct expenses or other costs incurred.
•
The current and previous billing period invoice amounts.
•
The budget spent, budget remaining, and cost to complete.
•
Backup documentation for all expenses.
The invoice amounts will be split by project in the event Consultant is working on more than one Project.
Task 1.2 – Risk Management
The Consultant will participate in risk reviews for the development and maintenance of a project risk register in 
coordination with the Program’s risk management team. A risk meeting will be held at the start of each phase of 
the project. Each meeting will be about one hour in length.
Task 1.3 – Quality Control
The Consultant will develop a Quality Assurance/Quality Control (QA/QC) Plan for implementation of the Scope of 
Work. The Consultant’s QA/QC Plan will be reviewed and approved by the City Project Manager and will include 
or reference all the controls necessary for implementation. 
62 of 207

--- page 63 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 11 of 21
Rev 4/26
Task 1.4 – Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors
If the Consultant requires the services of any subconsultants or subcontractors, then the Consultant will include 
a list of them in the proposal, explain why they are needed, and provide a general description of their effort.
The Consultant will manage the activities of subconsultants and subcontractors during performance of the project. 
All requirements of the contract shall also apply to subconsultants and subcontractors.
Task 1.5 – Agency Coordination
The Consultant will assist the City in coordinating the project with various local/county, state/regional, and 
federal agencies. This task includes attending meetings, as needed, with applicable agencies, providing 
meeting minutes, and providing project information and exhibits to the agencies. This task is established as 
an allowance task and shall only be used when specifically requested by the City in writing. Costs for this task 
shall be invoiced as a separate line item and shall include a copy of the authorization from the City.
Deliverables
Monthly progress reports, including progress narrative, invoices and financial details.
Task 2 – Construction Management Services
1.
Prepare and submit a Construction Management Plan prior to start of project work. This Plan is to be 
read in conjunction with Clean Water Program (CWP) Program Management Plan (PMP) and 
Construction Management Guidelines.
2.
Project and Contract Administration:
(a) Coordinate pre-construction and weekly progress meetings and prepare and distribute agenda and 
meeting minutes. The meeting minutes shall capture key points of information shared, including 
any issues discussed, directions given, decisions made, and action items assigned.
(b) Evaluate the Construction Contractor’s contract schedule for actual versus planned progress and 
document all scheduling discrepancies and deviation, including baseline schedule review and 
acceptance; monthly schedule update reviews; review and approval of schedule changes; and 
schedule recovery plans.
(c) Prepare and respond to Time Impact Analysis (TIA). 
(d) Communicate effectively and coordinate with Construction Contractor, Project Manager, Program 
Manager, Program Team, City Operations and Maintenance (O&M), Design Engineer, and Public 
Outreach Team.
(e) Coordinate project access to City O&M and Project Manager during Construction and Repair of 
Collection Sewers in City Streets.
(f) Assist Project Manager and City with Public outreach activities, including responding to community 
inquiries related to the project.
(g) Prepare and submit Construction monthly progress reports along with monthly payment invoice. 
(h) Construction Monthly progress reports shall include the following:
(i)
Overview of work accomplished, including photos;
(ii)
Safety Records;
(iii)
Outstanding and potential issues;
63 of 207

--- page 64 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 12 of 21
Rev 4/26
(iv)
Work to be completed the following month;
(v)
Updated schedule of work;
(vi)
Explanations for deviations from the initial schedule;
(vii)
Cost status and forecast;
(viii)
Potential changes and force accounts;
(ix)
Overview of quality of work;
(x)
Overview of project communications;
(xi)
Explanation of any upcoming risks/problem areas and corrective action to be taken.
(i) Review Construction Contractor’s conduct for compliance with contract documents, environmental 
documents, state laws, etc.
(j) Review Construction Contractor’s submittals for compliance with contract documents and 
coordinate with Program Manager, Project Manager, City and Design Engineer review.
(k) Review Construction Contractor’s Requests for Information (RFI) and coordinate with Project 
Manager, City and Design Engineer for resolution and response where appropriate. Log all RFIs and 
associated responses.
(l) Review, approve, and maintain Schedule of Values.
(m) Review Construction Contractor’s monthly invoices and recommend payment requests.
(n) Monitor and ensure the Contractor, including their Sub-Contractors’ upload monthly their Certified 
Payrolls to the State of California Department of Industrial Relations (DIR) Public Web Site to ensure 
compliance of the monthly hours worked by all craft and labor workforce onsite. This includes the 
General Contractor and Consultant’s craft/labor employees performing work onsite and their 
related work hours completed and reported each month. 
a.
For projects in which a third-party labor compliance firm is engaged, review monthly Labor 
Compliance Reports and coordinate resolution of potential issues.
(o) Provide Construction Contractor with acceptability or deficiencies in work and required actions for 
contract compliance.
(p) Provide information to Construction Contractor for its detailed and feasible system outage 
requests. 
(q) Review all shutdown requests (MOPs) and contingency plans for feasibility and reliability. 
Coordinate MOPs with Project Manager, City O&M, Design Engineer and other stakeholders.
(r) Coordinate all system outage requests and general construction activity with Construction 
Contractor, City Operations and Maintenance (O&M), Design Engineer and other stakeholders to 
ensure adequate operations of facilities.
(s) Maintain project document files.
(t) Track and manage all communications to assure response compliance.
(u) Prepare responses to correspondence in a timely manner.
(v) Utilize the Procore project documentation website furnished by the City of San Mateo.
(w) Monitor Contractor Quality Control Program.
(x) Prepare Non-Conformance Notice (NTC) and Coordinate Resolution.

Rev 4/26
(y) Coordinate with Public Works Personnel for review of any PLC/SCADA submittals against the Public 
Works SCADA standards, as well as for startup and testing support related to the SCADA standards 
as needed.
(z) Coordinate testing, startup and commissioning, including:
(xii)
Coordinate review and acceptance of Testing, Commissioning, and Startup Plans.
(xiii)
Monitor facility, equipment, SCADA and performance testing. Coordinate review and

(xv)
Conduct Witness Testing.
(xvi)
Review & Approve submitted test results.
(xvii)
Track deficient test results or repair work.
3.
Construction Observation/Inspection:
(a) Inspect and observe Construction Contractor’s work daily for compliance with the contract 
documents.
(b) Maintain daily inspection reports of work, including photos, descriptions of daily activities, any 
corrective actions to be taken by Construction Contractor, persons and equipment onsite, and work 
to be completed the following day(s). 
(c) Review materials and equipment delivered to site for compliance with submittals and contract 
documents.
(d) Coordinate material testing and specialty inspections (to be furnished by separate Consultant) as 
required in the contract documents.
(e) Review Construction Contractor’s compliance with all permits, stormwater regulations and permits 
(Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan [SWPPP]), environmental documents, and mitigation 
measures.
(f) Review Construction Contractor’s compliance with health and safety standards and regulations, 
and report non-compliance.
(g) Conduct monthly monitoring of record documents to ensure proper maintenance and completion 
by Contractor.
(h) Maintain Completeness of Field Hard Copy Files.
(i) Confirm that preliminary O&M Manuals have been submitted and approved prior to scheduling 
training on a piece of equipment.
(j) Confirm training is complete prior to scheduling the parties for startup and testing of equipment.  
4.
Change Order Management:
(a) Prepare and issue Field Orders (FO)/Work Change Directives (WCD).
(b) Prepare and issue Requests for Proposals (RFP).
(c) Coordinate, review, and evaluate potential change orders for entitlement and merit review and 
negotiate change order pricing.
65 of 207

--- page 66 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 14 of 21
Rev 4/26
(d) Maintain change order log for construction activities, and track and communicate potential 
upcoming changes.
(e) Prepare and update monthly Change and Payment Summary (CPS).
(f) Coordinate, review, evaluate, and recommend course of action for contract change order requests 
and submittals.
(g) Prepare Change Request Forms (CRF) and Contract Change Orders. 
(h) Conduct inspections and evaluate site conditions that are perceived to be different than shown in 
contract documents.
(i) Review, gather, and evaluate information for resolution of potential claims or disputes.
5.
Project Closeout and Acceptance Services
(a) Conduct site inspection(s) to determine if facilities are complete and in compliance with contract 
documents. Coordinate with Design Engineer and other required staff to walk the site for 
developing corrective items of work list.
(b) Develop corrective item work lists (punch lists) and conduct inspection of corrective actions 
performed. Monitor Construction Contractor’s correction of the items on the punch list for 
satisfactory completion.
(c) Consider and recommend final acceptance of work by the City.
(d) Recommend retention release to Construction Contractor.
(e) Prepare substantial completion certificate once corrective actions are complete and project is in 
compliance with contract documents to the satisfaction of the Program Manager.
(f) Verify all O&M Manual submittals as well as spare parts as required by the contract documents.
(g) Coordinate final submittal of organized and complete record drawings in accordance with contract 
documents.
(h) Confirm receipt of all special warranties, guarantees, spare parts, and additional maintenance 
materials.
(i) Review Construction Contractor as-builts and review for completeness and accuracy.
(j) Verify Construction Contractor has made all payments and that all required releases, including 
warranty release, lien release(s), and release of claims have been submitted by Construction 
Contractor.
(k) Prepare and submit final construction report. 
Task 3 – General Construction Management Services
The following general construction management related services may be included depending on the project and 
will be defined on an as-needed basis in the request for proposal for each project Task Order. 
1.
Participate in meetings with Program staff and other stakeholders, review available documents and 
document management system, and visit project sites to accomplish program and project 
familiarization.
66 of 207

--- page 67 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 15 of 21
Rev 4/26
2.
Perform as needed/miscellaneous preconstruction services as requested by Public Works Personnel up 
to the level of effort/budget established in this TO.
3.
Perform constructability reviews of Contract Documents including plans, specifications, and 
construction schedules and document the review findings in a form agreed with Public Works 
Personnel.
(a) The reviews shall consist of both intra-discipline and inter-discipline for Structural, Mechanical, 
Process, Civil, I&C, Electrical, Architectural and specialty disciplines such as Geotechnical and 
Environmental.
(b) If applicable, the Consultant shall make recommendations for cost-effective alternative materials, 
and/or design concepts.
(c) The review shall include but not be limited to the following:
(i)
Clarity, consistency, completeness, and ease of construction to achieve the overall project 
objectives.
(ii)
Detailed requirements of equipment procurement and fabrication.
(iii)
Identification of existing underground utilities.
(iv)
Detailed description of traffic control requirements, hours of work, trench closure 
procedures and maximum number of headings allowed. 
(v)
Detailed description of third-party coordination requirements (e.g. utilities, Right of Entry 
permits, and worker training needed for working within railroad right-of-way (ROW), or 
work within CALTRANS ROW)
(vi)
Detailed description of closure periods for work near businesses, schools, or other events.
(vii)
Identification of interfaces and potential interfaces between adjacent contracts and 
confirmation they are clear and constructible.
(viii)
Front ends and General Requirements for consistency across projects, compatibility with 
City and other regulatory requirements, incorporation of effective tools for administering 
the contract and adequate definition of temporary facilities to expedite mobilization 
(including CM facilities).
(ix)
Specific requirements for major schedule milestones within each Contract to help ensure 
coordination with other Contracts and prevent interruptions to existing operations.
(x)
Biddability to minimize change orders and risks of bid protest. Review of bid list to include: 
verify bid items and quantities; make sure the correct pay sections are referenced; and 
approve the units of measurements included in the list. Verify bid list for incorporation of 
allowances for common City practice for expediting the work, such as permit fees, field 
orders and third-party utility coordination/costs.
4.
Provide cost estimating and scheduling support, including review of Design Engineer opinions of 
probable construction cost and preliminary construction schedules.
5.
Provide as-needed CMS support under the direction of the CML including consultation, review of cost 
proposals/estimates, construction schedules and construction contract items. Said support will not be 
construed as taking on contractor responsibilities for construction means and methods, safety, budget, 
schedule, performance, etc.

EXHIBIT C
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS
MINIMUM SCOPE OF INSURANCE 
Coverage shall be at least as broad as:
1. Commercial General Liability (CGL):  Insurance Services Office (ISO) Form CG 00 01 12 07 
covering CGL on an “occurrence” basis, including products-completed operations, personal & 
advertising injury, with limits no less than $2,000,000 per occurrence. If a general aggregate limit 
applies, either the general aggregate limit shall apply separately to this project/location or the 
general aggregate limit shall be twice the required occurrence limit.
2. Automobile Liability:  ISO Form Number CA 00 01 covering any auto (Code 1), or if CONSULTANT 
has no owned autos, hired, (Code 8) and non-owned autos (Code 9), with limit no less than 
$1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury and property damage.
3. Workers’ Compensation: as required by the State of California, with Statutory Limits, and 
Employer’s Liability Insurance with limit of no less than $1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury 
or disease. 
4. Professional Liability (Errors and Omissions): Insurance appropriate to the CONSULTANT’s 
profession, with limit no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence or claim, $2,000,000 aggregate
If the CONSULTANT maintains higher limits than the minimums shown above, the City requires and shall 
be entitled to coverage for the higher limits maintained by the CONSULTANT.
Other Insurance Provisions
The insurance policies are to contain, or be endorsed to contain, the following provisions:
Additional Insured Status
The City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents are to be covered as 
insureds on the auto policy for liability arising out of automobiles owned, leased, hired or borrowed by 
or on behalf of the CONSULTANT; and on the CGL policy with respect to liability arising out of work or 
operations performed by or on behalf of the CONSULTANT including materials, parts or equipment 
furnished in connection with such work or operations. General liability coverage can be provided in the 
form of an endorsement to the CONSULTANT’s insurance (at least as broad as ISO Form CG 20 10, 11 
85 or both CG 20 10 and CG 20 37 forms if later revisions used).
Primary Coverage
For any claims related to this contract, the CONSULTANT’s insurance coverage shall be primary 
insurance as respects the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents. 
Any insurance or self-insurance maintained by the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, 
volunteers, or agents shall be excess of the CONSULTANT’s insurance and shall not contribute with it.
Notice of Cancellation
Each insurance policy required above shall provide that coverage shall not be canceled, except after 
thirty (30) days’ prior written notice (10 days for non-payment) has been given to the City. If a Notice

Agreement.

perform the services of CONSULTANT.

provisions of this Agreement.

SECTION 19 - MEDIATION
Should any dispute arise out of this Agreement, any party may request that it be 
submitted to mediation.  The parties shall meet in mediation within 30 days of a request.  The 
mediator shall be agreed to by the mediating parties; in the absence of an agreement, the 
parties shall each submit one name from mediators listed by either the American Arbitration 
Association, the State Mediation and Conciliation Service, or other agreed-upon service.  The 
mediator shall be selected by a blind draw.
The cost of mediation shall be borne equally by the parties.  Neither party shall be 
deemed the prevailing party.  No party shall be permitted to file a legal action without first 
meeting in mediation and making a good faith attempt to reach a mediated settlement.  The 
mediation process, once commenced by a meeting with the mediator, shall last until agreement 
is reached by the parties but not more than 60 days, unless the maximum time is extended by 
the parties.
SECTION 20 - LITIGATION
CONSULTANT shall testify at CITY'S request if litigation is brought against CITY in 
connection with CONSULTANT's services under this Agreement.  Unless the action is brought by 
CONSULTANT, or is based upon CONSULTANT's wrongdoing, CITY shall compensate 
CONSULTANT for preparation for testimony, testimony, and travel at CONSULTANT's standard 
hourly rates at the time of actual testimony.
