Scope of Services
Task 1 – General Services
Project management and coordination include tasks that will be performed by the Consultant throughout the 
project. 
These services include:
General Project Management and Coordination
Risk Management
Quality Control
Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors, if applicable
Agency Coordination and City Meeting Support
Task 1.1 – Project Management 
Task 1.1.1 – General Management
The Consultant will be responsible for detailed management of the project, including subconsultants and 
subcontractors, and will keep the City/Construction Management Lead (CML) appraised of the status of the 
project.
The Consultant will provide the key management personnel as described in their proposal on this project. The 
Consultant will not reassign the key project personnel without prior approval of the City/CML per the Standard 
Agreement for Professional Consultant Services.
Task 1.1.2 – Progress Report
The Consultant will prepare and submit monthly progress reports that include a narrative of the work completed 
by task and project invoicing. The invoices will comply with Program standards and document the following:
•
The man-hours and billing rates for staff for each task in the WBS
•
All direct expenses or other costs incurred.
•
The current and previous billing period invoice amounts.
•
The budget spent, budget remaining, and cost to complete.
•
Backup documentation for all expenses.
The invoice amounts will be split by project in the event Consultant is working on more than one Project.
Task 1.2 – Risk Management
The Consultant will participate in risk reviews for the development and maintenance of a project risk register in 
coordination with the Program’s risk management team. A risk meeting will be held at the start of each phase of 
the project. Each meeting will be about one hour in length.
Task 1.3 – Quality Control
The Consultant will develop a Quality Assurance/Quality Control (QA/QC) Plan for implementation of the Scope of 
Work. The Consultant’s QA/QC Plan will be reviewed and approved by the City Project Manager and will include 
or reference all the controls necessary for implementation. 
84 of 207

--- page 85 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 11 of 19
Rev 4/26
Task 1.4 – Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors
If the Consultant requires the services of any subconsultants or subcontractors, then the Consultant will include 
a list of them in the proposal, explain why they are needed, and provide a general description of their effort.
The Consultant will manage the activities of subconsultants and subcontractors during performance of the project. 
All requirements of the contract shall also apply to subconsultants and subcontractors.
Task 1.5 – Agency Coordination
The Consultant will assist the City in coordinating the project with various local/county, state/regional, and 
federal agencies. This task includes attending meetings, as needed, with applicable agencies, providing 
meeting minutes, and providing project information and exhibits to the agencies. This task is established as 
an allowance task and shall only be used when specifically requested by the City in writing. Costs for this task 
shall be invoiced as a separate line item and shall include a copy of the authorization from the City.
Deliverables
Monthly progress reports, including progress narrative, invoices and financial details.
Task 2 – Construction Management Services
1.
Prepare and submit a Construction Management Plan prior to start of project work. This Plan is to be 
read in conjunction with Clean Water Program (CWP) Program Management Plan (PMP) and 
Construction Management Guidelines.
2.
Project and Contract Administration:
(a) Coordinate pre-construction and weekly progress meetings and prepare and distribute agenda and 
meeting minutes. The meeting minutes shall capture key points of information shared, including 
any issues discussed, directions given, decisions made, and action items assigned.
(b) Evaluate the Construction Contractor’s contract schedule for actual versus planned progress and 
document all scheduling discrepancies and deviation, including baseline schedule review and 
acceptance; monthly schedule update reviews; review and approval of schedule changes; and 
schedule recovery plans.
(c) Prepare and respond to Time Impact Analysis (TIA). 
(d) Communicate effectively and coordinate with Construction Contractor, Project Manager, Program 
Manager, Program Team, City Operations and Maintenance (O&M), Design Engineer, and Public 
Outreach Team.
(e) Coordinate project access to City O&M and Project Manager during Construction and Repair of 
Collection Sewers in City Streets.
(f) Assist Project Manager and City with Public outreach activities, including responding to community 
inquiries related to the project.
(g) Prepare and submit Construction monthly progress reports along with monthly payment invoice. 
(h) Construction Monthly progress reports shall include the following:
(i)
Overview of work accomplished, including photos;
(ii)
Safety Records;
(iii)
Outstanding and potential issues;

(iv)
Work to be completed the following month;
(v)
Updated schedule of work;
(vi)

(vii)
Cost status and forecast;
(viii)
Potential changes and force accounts;
(ix)
Overview of quality of work;
(x)
Overview of project communications;
(xi)
Explanation of any upcoming risks/problem areas and corrective action to be taken.
(i) Review Construction Contractor’s conduct for compliance with contract documents, environmental 
documents, state laws, etc.
(j) Review Construction Contractor’s submittals for compliance with contract documents and 
coordinate with Program Manager, Project Manager, City and Design Engineer review.

Manager, City and Design Engineer for resolution and response where appropriate. Log all RFIs and 
associated responses.
(l) Review, approve, and maintain Schedule of Values.
(m) Review Construction Contractor’s monthly invoices and recommend payment requests.
(n) Monitor and ensure the Contractor, including their Sub-Contractors’ upload monthly their Certified 
Payrolls to the State of California Department of Industrial Relations (DIR) Public Web Site to ensure 
compliance of the monthly hours worked by all craft and labor workforce onsite. This includes the 
General Contractor and Consultant’s craft/labor employees performing work onsite and their

a.

Compliance Reports and coordinate resolution of potential issues.

requests.

ensure adequate operations of facilities.

(u) Prepare responses to correspondence in a timely manner.

performed. Monitor Construction Contractor’s correction of the items on the punch list for 
satisfactory completion.
(c) Consider and recommend final acceptance of work by the City.

materials.

(j) Verify Construction Contractor has made all payments and that all required releases, including 
warranty release, lien release(s), and release of claims have been submitted by Construction 
Contractor.
(k) Prepare and submit final construction report. 
Task 3 – General Construction Management Services
The following general construction management related services may be included depending on the project and 
will be defined on an as-needed basis in the request for proposal for each project Task Order. 
1.
Participate in meetings with Program staff and other stakeholders, review available documents and 
document management system, and visit project sites to accomplish program and project 
familiarization.
88 of 207

--- page 89 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 15 of 19
Rev 4/26
2.
Perform as needed/miscellaneous preconstruction services as requested by Public Works Personnel up 
to the level of effort/budget established in this TO.
3.
Perform constructability reviews of Contract Documents including plans, specifications, and 
construction schedules and document the review findings in a form agreed with Public Works 
Personnel.
(a) The reviews shall consist of both intra-discipline and inter-discipline for Structural, Mechanical, 
Process, Civil, I&C, Electrical, Architectural and specialty disciplines such as Geotechnical and 
Environmental.
(b) If applicable, the Consultant shall make recommendations for cost-effective alternative materials, 
and/or design concepts.
(c) The review shall include but not be limited to the following:
(i)
Clarity, consistency, completeness, and ease of construction to achieve the overall project 
objectives.
(ii)
Detailed requirements of equipment procurement and fabrication.
(iii)
Identification of existing underground utilities.
(iv)
Detailed description of traffic control requirements, hours of work, trench closure 
procedures and maximum number of headings allowed. 
(v)
Detailed description of third-party coordination requirements (e.g. utilities, Right of Entry 
permits, and worker training needed for working within railroad right-of-way (ROW), or 
work within CALTRANS ROW)
(vi)
Detailed description of closure periods for work near businesses, schools, or other events.
(vii)
Identification of interfaces and potential interfaces between adjacent contracts and 
confirmation they are clear and constructible.
(viii)
Front ends and General Requirements for consistency across projects, compatibility with 
City and other regulatory requirements, incorporation of effective tools for administering 
the contract and adequate definition of temporary facilities to expedite mobilization 
(including CM facilities).
(ix)
Specific requirements for major schedule milestones within each Contract to help ensure 
coordination with other Contracts and prevent interruptions to existing operations.
(x)
Biddability to minimize change orders and risks of bid protest. Review of bid list to include: 
verify bid items and quantities; make sure the correct pay sections are referenced; and 
approve the units of measurements included in the list. Verify bid list for incorporation of 
allowances for common City practice for expediting the work, such as permit fees, field 
orders and third-party utility coordination/costs.
4.
Provide cost estimating and scheduling support, including review of Design Engineer opinions of 
probable construction cost and preliminary construction schedules.
5.
Provide as-needed CMS support under the direction of the CML including consultation, review of cost 
proposals/estimates, construction schedules and construction contract items. Said support will not be 
construed as taking on contractor responsibilities for construction means and methods, safety, budget, 
schedule, performance, etc.

BASIS OF CHARGES
A.
Rates shown assume projects under this on-call contract will require compliance with California Prevailing Wage rate 
requirements and assumes the Client will be filing a PWC-100 Form to the California Department of Industrial Relations (DIR) 
for the project.
B.
Rates for prevailing wage categories are subject to annual escalations in accordance with the bi-annual wage determinations from 
the California DIR. Rates based on California DIR’s wage determinations dated February 2026.
C.
Per the requirements being enforced under SB 854 and because it is assumed that a PWC-100 Form will be filed by the Client to 
the CA DIR for each project, 4LEAF is required to notify an authorized Apprenticeship Committee through submittal of a DAS-140 
form. We are then required to make an official request to an authorized Apprenticeship Committee for an apprentice by 
submitting a DAS-142 form. We are not assured the apprenticeship committee will be able to provide a suitable/qualified 
apprentice for the project. Per the apprenticeship requirements, the hours worked by the apprentice must be in a ratio of 1:5 
for apprentice to journeyman hours. 4LEAF will not know the labor classification of the Public Works Apprentice until an 
Apprentice is dispatched to the site; therefore, the rates for the five Periods listed under the California DIR’s Wage determination 
for Building Construction Inspector issued February 2025 were used to determine the range of Calendar Year 2025 hourly rates 
for Public Works Inspector Apprentice.
D.
All invoicing will be submitted monthly.
E.
Overtime and Premium time will be charged as follows:
-
Night Time (work begun after 4PM or before 5AM)
1.125 x hourly rate
-
Overtime 1 (over 8 hours M-F or Saturdays)
1.35 x hourly rate
-
Overtime 2 (over 8 hours Sat or 1st 8 hour Sun)
1.85 x hourly rate
-
Overtime 3 (over 8 hours Sun or Holidays)
2.35 x hourly rate
F.
All work with less than 8 hours rest between shifts will be charged the appropriate overtime rate.
G.
Subconsultant invoices will be assessed a 10% Administrative Processing Fee.
H.
Project-related mileage for inspections will be billed at the allowable IRS Rate.
I.
Payment due on receipt. All payments over 30 days will be assessed as a 1.5% interest charge.
J.
Client shall pay attorneys’ fees or other costs incurred in collecting delinquent amounts.
K.
Client agrees that 4LEAF’s liability will be limited to the value of services provided.
L.
In accordance with California’s Meal Break and Rest Break Law requirements, Client will be billed one (1) additional hour per 
day at the regular rate for each missed meal or rest break due to Client-directed tasks or requirements. Client should allow 
4LEAF’s non-exempt, hourly employees the opportunity to take their entitled rest and meal breaks during each work shift.
M.
If 4LEAF is requested or otherwise required to conform to Client’s alternative workweek schedule (“AWW”), Client hereby agrees 
to compensate or reimburse 4LEAF for all overtime paid to its employees who work an AWW. If 4LEAF’s affected employment 
group approves an AWW election and the same is registered, overtime compensation/reimbursement shall not be required.

INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS
MINIMUM SCOPE OF INSURANCE 
Coverage shall be at least as broad as:
1. Commercial General Liability (CGL):  Insurance Services Office (ISO) Form CG 00 01 12 07 
covering CGL on an “occurrence” basis, including products-completed operations, personal & 
advertising injury, with limits no less than $2,000,000 per occurrence. If a general aggregate limit 
applies, either the general aggregate limit shall apply separately to this project/location or the 
general aggregate limit shall be twice the required occurrence limit.
2. Automobile Liability:  ISO Form Number CA 00 01 covering any auto (Code 1), or if CONSULTANT 
has no owned autos, hired, (Code 8) and non-owned autos (Code 9), with limit no less than 
$1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury and property damage.
3. Workers’ Compensation: as required by the State of California, with Statutory Limits, and 
Employer’s Liability Insurance with limit of no less than $1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury 
or disease. 
4. Professional Liability (Errors and Omissions): Insurance appropriate to the CONSULTANT’s 
profession, with limit no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence or claim, $2,000,000 aggregate
If the CONSULTANT maintains higher limits than the minimums shown above, the City requires and shall 
be entitled to coverage for the higher limits maintained by the CONSULTANT.
Other Insurance Provisions
The insurance policies are to contain, or be endorsed to contain, the following provisions:
Additional Insured Status
The City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents are to be covered as 
insureds on the auto policy for liability arising out of automobiles owned, leased, hired or borrowed by 
or on behalf of the CONSULTANT; and on the CGL policy with respect to liability arising out of work or 
operations performed by or on behalf of the CONSULTANT including materials, parts or equipment 
furnished in connection with such work or operations. General liability coverage can be provided in the 
form of an endorsement to the CONSULTANT’s insurance (at least as broad as ISO Form CG 20 10, 11 
85 or both CG 20 10 and CG 20 37 forms if later revisions used).
Primary Coverage
For any claims related to this contract, the CONSULTANT’s insurance coverage shall be primary 
insurance as respects the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents. 
Any insurance or self-insurance maintained by the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, 
volunteers, or agents shall be excess of the CONSULTANT’s insurance and shall not contribute with it.
Notice of Cancellation
Each insurance policy required above shall provide that coverage shall not be canceled, except after 
thirty (30) days’ prior written notice (10 days for non-payment) has been given to the City. If a Notice 
of Cancellation endorsement is not available, CONSULTANT shall ensure the requisite Notice of 
Cancellation is specified in the Certificate of Insurance.  In the event the Notice of Cancellation is not 
92 of 207

--- page 93 ---

Page 19 of 19
Rev 4/26
specified in the Certificate of Insurance, Consultant shall be responsible for providing the City the 
requisite notice as specified in this Exhibit C. 
Waiver of Subrogation
CONSULTANT hereby grants to City a waiver of any right to subrogation which any insurer of said 
CONSULTANT may acquire against the City by virtue of the payment of any loss under such insurance.  
CONSULTANT agrees to obtain any endorsement that may be necessary to effect this waiver of 
subrogation, but this provision applies regardless of whether or not the City has received a waiver of 
subrogation endorsement from the insurer.  
Deductibles and Self-Insured Retentions
Any deductibles or self-insured retentions must be declared to and approved by the City. The City may 
require the CONSULTANT to purchase coverage with a lower deductible or retention or provide proof 
of ability to pay losses and related investigations, claim administration, and defense expenses within 
the retention.  
Acceptability of Insurers
Insurance is to be placed with insurers with a current A.M. Best’s rating of no less than A:VII, unless 
otherwise acceptable to the City.
Verification of Coverage
CONSULTANT shall furnish the City with original certificates and amendatory endorsements or copies 
of the applicable policy language effecting coverage required by this clause.  All certificates and 
endorsements are to be received and approved by the City before work commences.  However, failure 
to obtain the required documents prior to the work beginning shall not waive the CONSULTANT’s 
obligation to provide them.  The City reserves the right to require complete, certified copies of all 
required insurance policies, including endorsements required by these specifications, at any time.

AGREEMENT WITH MARINA CONSTRUCTION MANAGEMENT, INC.
FOR PROFESSIONAL CONSULTANT SERVICES
FOR 
POST-CLEAN WATER PROGRAM CONSULTING SERVICES FOR AS-NEEDED COLLECTION SYSTEM 
CONSTRUCTION MANAGEMENT SERVICES
This Agreement, made and entered into this day of ____________________, by and 
between the CITY OF SAN MATEO, a municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State 
of California (“CITY”), and Marina Construction Management, Inc., a corporation, 
(“CONSULTANT”), whose address is 650 Castro Street, Suite 120-492, Mountain View, CA 
94041.
RECITALS:
A.  CITY desires certain professional services hereinafter described.
B.  CITY desires to engage CONSULTANT to provide these professional services by 
reason of its qualifications and experience for performing such services and CONSULTANT has 
offered to provide the required services on the terms and in the manner set forth herein.
NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS AGREED as follows:
SECTION 1 - SCOPE OF SERVICES
The scope of services to be performed by CONSULTANT under this Agreement is as 
described in Exhibit A to this Agreement, attached and incorporated by reference.
SECTION 2 - DUTIES OF CONSULTANT
CONSULTANT shall be responsible for the professional quality, technical accuracy and 
coordination of all work furnished by CONSULTANT under this Agreement.  CONSULTANT shall, 
without additional compensation, correct or revise any errors or deficiencies in its work.
CONSULTANT represents that it is qualified to furnish the services described under this 
Agreement.
CONSULTANT shall be responsible for employing or engaging all persons necessary to 
perform the services of CONSULTANT.
CONSULTANT agrees to comply with the City’s minimum wage ordinance as codified in 
Municipal Code Chapter 5.92, which differs from the state minimum wage and includes a 
Consumer Price Index escalator.

--- page 95 ---

Rev 4/26
SECTION 3 - DUTIES OF CITY
CITY shall provide pertinent information regarding its requirements for the project.
CITY shall examine documents submitted by CONSULTANT and shall render decisions 
pertaining thereto promptly, to avoid unreasonable delay in the progress of CONSULTANT's 
work.
SECTION 4 - TERM
This Agreement shall be valid for a duration of five (5) years. The services to be 
performed under this Agreement shall commence upon execution of the Agreement and be 
completed on June 30, 2031.  
SECTION 5 - PAYMENT
Payment shall be made by CITY only for services rendered and upon submission of a 
payment request upon completion and CITY approval of the work performed.  In consideration 
for the full performance of the services set forth in Exhibit A, CITY agrees to pay CONSULTANT a 
fee in an amount of $3,000,000, pursuant to rates stated in Exhibit B to this Agreement, 
attached and incorporated by reference.
SECTION 6 - TERMINATION
Without limitation to such rights or remedies as CITY shall otherwise have by law, CITY 
shall have the right to terminate this Agreement or suspend work on the Project for any reason, 
upon ten (10) days' written notice to CONSULTANT.  CONSULTANT agrees to cease all work under 
this Agreement upon receipt of said written notice.
Upon termination and upon CITY's payment of the amount required to be paid, 
documents become the property of CITY, and CONSULTANT shall transfer them to CITY upon 
request without additional compensation.
SECTION 7 - OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS
All documents prepared by CONSULTANT in the performance of this Agreement, 
although instruments of professional service, are and shall be the property of CITY, whether the 
project for which they are made is executed or not.

All reports and documents prepared by CONSULTANT in connection with the 
performance of this Agreement are confidential until released by CITY to the public.

It is expressly agreed that in the performance of the professional services required

officials, employees, volunteers, and agents from and against any and all claims, loss, liability,

defend City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents against any 
such claims. The CONSULTANT’S duty to indemnify shall survive expiration or early termination 
of this Agreement.

SECTION 13 - NON-ASSIGNABILITY
Both parties hereto recognize that this Agreement is for the personal services of

the prior written consent of CITY.

If any term or portion of this Agreement is held to be invalid, illegal, or otherwise 
unenforceable by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remaining provisions of this Agreement 
shall continue in full force and effect.
SECTION 17 - COSTS AND ATTORNEY FEES
Attorney fees in total amount not exceeding $5000, shall be recoverable as costs (by the 
filing of a cost bill) by the prevailing party in any action or actions to enforce the provisions of 
this Agreement.  The above $5000 limit is the total of attorney fees recoverable whether in the 
trial court, appellate court, or otherwise, and regardless of the number of attorneys, trials, 
appeals, or actions.  It is the intent of this Agreement that neither party shall have to pay the 
other more than $5000 for attorney fees arising out of an action, or actions to enforce the 
provisions of this Agreement.
SECTION 18 - NON-DISCRIMINATION

color, national origin, ancestry, physical handicap, medical condition, marital status, sex, or age, 
unless based upon a bona fide occupational qualification pursuant to the California Fair 
Employment and Housing Act.

SECTION 19 - MEDIATION
Should any dispute arise out of this Agreement, any party may request that it be 
submitted to mediation.  The parties shall meet in mediation within 30 days of a request.  The 
mediator shall be agreed to by the mediating parties; in the absence of an agreement, the 
parties shall each submit one name from mediators listed by either the American Arbitration 
Association, the State Mediation and Conciliation Service, or other agreed-upon service.  The 
mediator shall be selected by a blind draw.
The cost of mediation shall be borne equally by the parties.  Neither party shall be 
deemed the prevailing party.  No party shall be permitted to file a legal action without first 
meeting in mediation and making a good faith attempt to reach a mediated settlement.  The 
mediation process, once commenced by a meeting with the mediator, shall last until agreement 
is reached by the parties but not more than 60 days, unless the maximum time is extended by 
the parties.
SECTION 20 - LITIGATION
CONSULTANT shall testify at CITY'S request if litigation is brought against CITY in 
connection with CONSULTANT's services under this Agreement.  Unless the action is brought by 
CONSULTANT, or is based upon CONSULTANT's wrongdoing, CITY shall compensate 
CONSULTANT for preparation for testimony, testimony, and travel at CONSULTANT's standard 
hourly rates at the time of actual testimony.
SECTION 21 - NOTICES

Scope of Services
Task 1 – General Services
Project management and coordination include tasks that will be performed by the Consultant throughout the 
project. 
These services include:
General Project Management and Coordination
Risk Management
Quality Control
Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors, if applicable
Agency Coordination and City Meeting Support
Task 1.1 – Project Management 
Task 1.1.1 – General Management
The Consultant will be responsible for detailed management of the project, including subconsultants and 
subcontractors, and will keep the City/Construction Management Lead (CML) appraised of the status of the 
project.
The Consultant will provide the key management personnel as described in their proposal on this project. The 
Consultant will not reassign the key project personnel without prior approval of the City/CML per the Standard 
Agreement for Professional Consultant Services.
Task 1.1.2 – Progress Report
The Consultant will prepare and submit monthly progress reports that include a narrative of the work completed 
by task and project invoicing. The invoices will comply with Program standards and document the following:
•
The man-hours and billing rates for staff for each task in the WBS
•
All direct expenses or other costs incurred.
•
The current and previous billing period invoice amounts.
•
The budget spent, budget remaining, and cost to complete.
•
Backup documentation for all expenses.
The invoice amounts will be split by project in the event Consultant is working on more than one Project.
Task 1.2 – Risk Management
The Consultant will participate in risk reviews for the development and maintenance of a project risk register in 
coordination with the Program’s risk management team. A risk meeting will be held at the start of each phase of 
the project. Each meeting will be about one hour in length.
Task 1.3 – Quality Control
The Consultant will develop a Quality Assurance/Quality Control (QA/QC) Plan for implementation of the Scope of 
Work. The Consultant’s QA/QC Plan will be reviewed and approved by the City Project Manager and will include 
or reference all the controls necessary for implementation. 
103 of 207

--- page 104 ---
Design Professional Services 2024
Page 11 of 20
Rev 4/26
Task 1.4 – Management of Subconsultants and Subcontractors
If the Consultant requires the services of any subconsultants or subcontractors, then the Consultant will include 
a list of them in the proposal, explain why they are needed, and provide a general description of their effort.
The Consultant will manage the activities of subconsultants and subcontractors during performance of the project. 
All requirements of the contract shall also apply to subconsultants and subcontractors.
Task 1.5 – Agency Coordination
The Consultant will assist the City in coordinating the project with various local/county, state/regional, and 
federal agencies. This task includes attending meetings, as needed, with applicable agencies, providing 
meeting minutes, and providing project information and exhibits to the agencies. This task is established as 
an allowance task and shall only be used when specifically requested by the City in writing. Costs for this task 
shall be invoiced as a separate line item and shall include a copy of the authorization from the City.
Deliverables
Monthly progress reports, including progress narrative, invoices and financial details.
Task 2 – Construction Management Services
1.
Prepare and submit a Construction Management Plan prior to start of project work. This Plan is to be 
read in conjunction with Clean Water Program (CWP) Program Management Plan (PMP) and 
Construction Management Guidelines.
2.
Project and Contract Administration:
(a) Coordinate pre-construction and weekly progress meetings and prepare and distribute agenda and 
meeting minutes. The meeting minutes shall capture key points of information shared, including 
any issues discussed, directions given, decisions made, and action items assigned.
(b) Evaluate the Construction Contractor’s contract schedule for actual versus planned progress and 
document all scheduling discrepancies and deviation, including baseline schedule review and 
acceptance; monthly schedule update reviews; review and approval of schedule changes; and 
schedule recovery plans.
(c) Prepare and respond to Time Impact Analysis (TIA). 
(d) Communicate effectively and coordinate with Construction Contractor, Project Manager, Program 
Manager, Program Team, City Operations and Maintenance (O&M), Design Engineer, and Public 
Outreach Team.
(e) Coordinate project access to City O&M and Project Manager during Construction and Repair of 
Collection Sewers in City Streets.
(f) Assist Project Manager and City with Public outreach activities, including responding to community 
inquiries related to the project.
(g) Prepare and submit Construction monthly progress reports along with monthly payment invoice. 
(h) Construction Monthly progress reports shall include the following:
(i)
Overview of work accomplished, including photos;
(ii)
Safety Records;
(iii)
Outstanding and potential issues;

associated responses.
(l) Review, approve, and maintain Schedule of Values.

compliance of the monthly hours worked by all craft and labor workforce onsite. This includes the 
General Contractor and Consultant’s craft/labor employees performing work onsite and their 
related work hours completed and reported each month. 
a.

ensure adequate operations of facilities.
(s) Maintain project document files.

(u) Prepare responses to correspondence in a timely manner.

(y) Coordinate with Public Works Personnel for review of any PLC/SCADA submittals against the Public

3.
Construction Observation/Inspection:
(a) Inspect and observe Construction Contractor’s work daily for compliance with the contract 
documents.
(b) Maintain daily inspection reports of work, including photos, descriptions of daily activities, any 
corrective actions to be taken by Construction Contractor, persons and equipment onsite, and work 
to be completed the following day(s). 
(c) Review materials and equipment delivered to site for compliance with submittals and contract 
documents.
(d) Coordinate material testing and specialty inspections (to be furnished by separate Consultant) as 
required in the contract documents.
(e) Review Construction Contractor’s compliance with all permits, stormwater regulations and permits 
(Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan [SWPPP]), environmental documents, and mitigation 
measures.
(f) Review Construction Contractor’s compliance with health and safety standards and regulations, 
and report non-compliance.
(g) Conduct monthly monitoring of record documents to ensure proper maintenance and completion 
by Contractor.
(h) Maintain Completeness of Field Hard Copy Files.
(i) Confirm that preliminary O&M Manuals have been submitted and approved prior to scheduling 
training on a piece of equipment.
(j) Confirm training is complete prior to scheduling the parties for startup and testing of equipment.  
4.
Change Order Management:
(a) Prepare and issue Field Orders (FO)/Work Change Directives (WCD).
(b) Prepare and issue Requests for Proposals (RFP).
(c) Coordinate, review, and evaluate potential change orders for entitlement and merit review and 
negotiate change order pricing.

Page 14 of 20
Rev 4/26
(d) Maintain change order log for construction activities, and track and communicate potential 
upcoming changes.
(e) Prepare and update monthly Change and Payment Summary (CPS).
(f) Coordinate, review, evaluate, and recommend course of action for contract change order requests 
and submittals.
(g) Prepare Change Request Forms (CRF) and Contract Change Orders. 
(h) Conduct inspections and evaluate site conditions that are perceived to be different than shown in 
contract documents.
(i) Review, gather, and evaluate information for resolution of potential claims or disputes.
5.
Project Closeout and Acceptance Services
(a) Conduct site inspection(s) to determine if facilities are complete and in compliance with contract 
documents. Coordinate with Design Engineer and other required staff to walk the site for 
developing corrective items of work list.
(b) Develop corrective item work lists (punch lists) and conduct inspection of corrective actions 
performed. Monitor Construction Contractor’s correction of the items on the punch list for 
satisfactory completion.
(c) Consider and recommend final acceptance of work by the City.
(d) Recommend retention release to Construction Contractor.
(e) Prepare substantial completion certificate once corrective actions are complete and project is in 
compliance with contract documents to the satisfaction of the Program Manager.
(f) Verify all O&M Manual submittals as well as spare parts as required by the contract documents.
(g) Coordinate final submittal of organized and complete record drawings in accordance with contract 
documents.
(h) Confirm receipt of all special warranties, guarantees, spare parts, and additional maintenance 
materials.
(i) Review Construction Contractor as-builts and review for completeness and accuracy.
(j) Verify Construction Contractor has made all payments and that all required releases, including 
warranty release, lien release(s), and release of claims have been submitted by Construction 
Contractor.
(k) Prepare and submit final construction report. 
Task 3 – General Construction Management Services
The following general construction management related services may be included depending on the project and 
will be defined on an as-needed basis in the request for proposal for each project Task Order. 
1.
Participate in meetings with Program staff and other stakeholders, review available documents and 
document management system, and visit project sites to accomplish program and project 
familiarization.

Rev 4/26
2.
Perform as needed/miscellaneous preconstruction services as requested by Public Works Personnel up 
to the level of effort/budget established in this TO.
3.
Perform constructability reviews of Contract Documents including plans, specifications, and 
construction schedules and document the review findings in a form agreed with Public Works 
Personnel.
(a) The reviews shall consist of both intra-discipline and inter-discipline for Structural, Mechanical, 
Process, Civil, I&C, Electrical, Architectural and specialty disciplines such as Geotechnical and 
Environmental.
(b) If applicable, the Consultant shall make recommendations for cost-effective alternative materials,

(i)
Clarity, consistency, completeness, and ease of construction to achieve the overall project 
objectives.
(ii)
Detailed requirements of equipment procurement and fabrication.
(iii)
Identification of existing underground utilities.
(iv)
Detailed description of traffic control requirements, hours of work, trench closure 
procedures and maximum number of headings allowed. 
(v)
Detailed description of third-party coordination requirements (e.g. utilities, Right of Entry 
permits, and worker training needed for working within railroad right-of-way (ROW), or 
work within CALTRANS ROW)
(vi)
Detailed description of closure periods for work near businesses, schools, or other events.
(vii)

confirmation they are clear and constructible.
(viii)
Front ends and General Requirements for consistency across projects, compatibility with 
City and other regulatory requirements, incorporation of effective tools for administering 
the contract and adequate definition of temporary facilities to expedite mobilization 
(including CM facilities).
(ix)
Specific requirements for major schedule milestones within each Contract to help ensure 
coordination with other Contracts and prevent interruptions to existing operations.
(x)
Biddability to minimize change orders and risks of bid protest. Review of bid list to include: 
verify bid items and quantities; make sure the correct pay sections are referenced; and 
approve the units of measurements included in the list. Verify bid list for incorporation of 
allowances for common City practice for expediting the work, such as permit fees, field 
orders and third-party utility coordination/costs.
4.
Provide cost estimating and scheduling support, including review of Design Engineer opinions of 
probable construction cost and preliminary construction schedules.
5.
Provide as-needed CMS support under the direction of the CML including consultation, review of cost 
proposals/estimates, construction schedules and construction contract items. Said support will not be 
construed as taking on contractor responsibilities for construction means and methods, safety, budget, 
schedule, performance, etc.

EXHIBIT C
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS
MINIMUM SCOPE OF INSURANCE 
Coverage shall be at least as broad as:
1. Commercial General Liability (CGL):  Insurance Services Office (ISO) Form CG 00 01 12 07 
covering CGL on an “occurrence” basis, including products-completed operations, personal & 
advertising injury, with limits no less than $2,000,000 per occurrence. If a general aggregate 
limit applies, either the general aggregate limit shall apply separately to this project/location or 
the general aggregate limit shall be twice the required occurrence limit.
2. Automobile Liability:  ISO Form Number CA 00 01 covering any auto (Code 1), or if 
CONSULTANT has no owned autos, hired, (Code 8) and non-owned autos (Code 9), with limit no 
less than $1,000,000 per accident for bodily injury and property damage.
3. Workers’ Compensation: as required by the State of California, with Statutory Limits, and 
Employer’s Liability Insurance with limit of no less than $1,000,000 per accident for bodily 
injury or disease. 
4. Professional Liability (Errors and Omissions): Insurance appropriate to the CONSULTANT’s 
profession, with limit no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence or claim, $2,000,000 aggregate.
If the CONSULTANT maintains higher limits than the minimums shown above, the City requires and 
shall be entitled to coverage for the higher limits maintained by the CONSULTANT.
Other Insurance Provisions
The insurance policies are to contain, or be endorsed to contain, the following provisions:
Additional Insured Status
The City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents are to be covered as 
insureds on the auto policy for liability arising out of automobiles owned, leased, hired or borrowed by 
or on behalf of the CONSULTANT; and on the CGL policy with respect to liability arising out of work or 
operations performed by or on behalf of the CONSULTANT including materials, parts or equipment 
furnished in connection with such work or operations. General liability coverage can be provided in the 
form of an endorsement to the CONSULTANT’s insurance (at least as broad as ISO Form CG 20 10, 11 
85 or both CG 20 10 and CG 20 37 forms if later revisions used).
Primary Coverage
For any claims related to this contract, the CONSULTANT’s insurance coverage shall be primary 
insurance as respects the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, volunteers, and agents. 
Any insurance or self-insurance maintained by the City, its elected and appointed officials, employees, 
volunteers, or agents shall be excess of the CONSULTANT’s insurance and shall not contribute with it.

Notice of Cancellation
Each insurance policy required above shall provide that coverage shall not be canceled, except after 
thirty (30) days’ prior written notice (10 days for non-payment) has been given to the City.
Waiver of Subrogation
CONSULTANT hereby grants to City a waiver of any right to subrogation which any insurer of said 
CONSULTANT may acquire against the City by virtue of the payment of any loss under such insurance.  
CONSULTANT agrees to obtain any endorsement that may be necessary to effect this waiver of 
subrogation, but this provision applies regardless of whether or not the City has received a waiver of 
subrogation endorsement from the insurer.  
Deductibles and Self-Insured Retentions
Any deductibles or self-insured retentions must be declared to and approved by the City. The City may 
require the CONSULTANT to purchase coverage with a lower deductible or retention or provide proof 
of ability to pay losses and related investigations, claim administration, and defense expenses within 
the retention.  
Acceptability of Insurers
Insurance is to be placed with insurers with a current A.M. Best’s rating of no less than A:VII, unless 
otherwise acceptable to the City.
Verification of Coverage
CONSULTANT shall furnish the City with original certificates and amendatory endorsements or copies 
of the applicable policy language effecting coverage required by this clause.  All certificates and 
endorsements are to be received and approved by the City before work commences.  However, failure 
to obtain the required documents prior to the work beginning shall not waive the CONSULTANT’s 
obligation to provide them.  The City reserves the right to require complete, certified copies of all 
required insurance policies, including endorsements required by these specifications, at any time.

SUBJECT:
Child Care Facility Fund Program Loan Documents – Revision and Amendment 
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve a Loan Agreement Amendment, Deed of Trust, and Promissory Note with Mighty Tiger Preschool, LLC to change 
the real property loan collateral for the Child Care Facilities Fund loan in the remaining amount of $344,000; and authorize 
City Manager to execute the Loan Agreement Amendment, Deed of Trust, and Promissory Note in substantially the form 
presented.
BACKGROUND:
The Child Care Facilities Fund was originally established in 2004 with the Child Care Development Fee to address the 
critical need for more childcare spaces in the City. There has been a longtime need for greater amount of information and 
assistance for potential childcare providers who are interested in starting or expanding their businesses. For those 
interested in obtaining financial assistance, the City has a variety of methods. To date, the primary method has been 
secured loans. The funds are legally restricted to capital expenditures for child care uses related to construction and 
elements that are fixed in the property. These loans are available to childcare providers and/or developers to specifically 
develop, expand, or otherwise enhance childcare facilities resulting in new licensed child care spaces. The benefits to the 
loan option are that the terms include zero percent interest, deferred/no payments, and a loan forgiveness schedule based 
on the amount of money borrowed. The loan forgiveness is contingent upon consistently meeting the annual performance 
requirements. 
On December 4, 2023, the City Council approved a Child Care Facility Fund loan of $430,000 to Geoffrey Choy and Ruth H. 
Choy (Owners) for tenant improvements at 2575 Flores Street, Suite 1, to establish a new child care facility, Mighty Tiger 
Preschool, LLC, resulting in 48 new licensed childcare spaces. Since then, the Owners have operated the Mighty Tiger 
Preschool, LLC, successfully and have qualified twice for the annual ten percent (10%) loan forgiveness of $43,000 for the 
years of 2024 and 2025. The annual ten percent (10%) loan forgiveness, as established in the City’s loan program, is 
contingent on the submission of an annual report demonstrating implementation of program requirements for the past 
year.  This annual report includes a narrative summarizing enrollment details to demonstrate that the business is operating 
per their approved scope and narratives summarizing the business’s annual efforts, highlights, challenges and professional 
development for staff that support quality and continuous improvement. A total of $86,000 has been forgiven from their 
original loan, and $344,000 remains outstanding. 
Earlier this year, due to the pending sale of the Owners’ property that was originally used as collateral for securing the 
loan, the Owners submitted a request to change the collateral property securing the remaining loan funds. The original 
property securing the loan, located at 115 Agustin Narvaez St #4, San Jose, CA 95136, was sold in April 2026. Due to delays 
with the Owners’ purchase of the new property, the remaining unforgiven $344,000 loan funds were issued back to the 
City to hold in an escrow account until the new property that would serve as collateral came under full legal ownership of 
115 of 207

--- page 116 ---
CITY OF SAN MATEO
the Owners. The proposed collateral property is located at 400 El Camino Real #210, Belmont, CA 94002, and it meets the 
minimum valuation requirements to secure the outstanding loan. This requested change has been reviewed by staff, 
meets all applicable requirements, and does not change the monetary terms under which the original loan was granted in 
2023. The current change only relates to the collateral property. If the Owners continue to successfully operate the 
preschool and qualify for the annual forgiveness at the regular rate of ten percent (10%) there will be an annual 
forgiveness of $43,000 based on the original loan amount of $430,000.
BUDGET IMPACT:
This is an existing loan from the Child Care Facilities Fund which was approved in 2023 and this change to the loan 
collateral does not have any impact to the Child Care Facilities Fund or the General Fund. 
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This loan collateral change is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it will not cause a 
physical change in the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)
STRATEGIC PLAN ALIGNMENT:
Administration of the Child Care Facility Fund Program supports the Land Use and Economic Development initiatives.
NOTICE PROVIDED:
All meeting noticing requirements were met.

PROMISSORY NOTE
(San Mateo-Child Care Loan Program)
$344,000
San Mateo, California
September __, 2026
FOR VALUE RECEIVED, MIGHTY TIGER PRESCHOOL, a California Limited 
Liability Company ("Borrower"), promises to pay to the City of San Mateo, a California 
municipal corporation ("Holder"), the principal sum of three hundred forty-four thousand 
Dollars ($344,000) plus interest thereon pursuant to Section 2 below. All capitalized terms used 
but not defined in this Promissory Note (the "Note") have the meanings set forth in the Loan 
Agreement (as defined below).   
 
1.
Borrower's Obligation.  This Note evidences the Borrower's obligation to pay the 
Holder the principal amount of three hundred forty-four thousand Dollars ($344,000) loaned 
to Borrower by Holder pursuant to that certain Loan Agreement between Borrower and Holder 
of even date herewith (the "Loan Agreement"). 
2.
Interest.  Except as otherwise set forth herein, this Note does not bear interest. If a 
Default occurs, interest on the principal balance will begin to accrue as of the date of Default 
(following expiration of applicable notice and cure periods) and continuing until such time as the 
Loan funds are repaid in full or the Default is cured, at which time the accrued interest shall be 
automatically forgiven at the default rate of the lesser of ten percent (10%), compounded 
annually, or the highest rate permitted by law. 
3.
Term and Repayment Requirements.  Annual forgiveness and payments due 
under this Note, if any, are due in accordance with this Note and as set forth in Section 2.7 of the 
Loan Agreement. 
4.
No Assumption.  This Note is not assumable by the successors and assigns of 
Borrower without the prior written consent of the Holder, except as may be otherwise set forth in 
the Loan Agreement.
5.
Security.  This Note is secured by that certain Short Form Deed of Trust and 
Assignment of Rents (the "Deed of Trust") wherein the Borrower is the trustor and the Holder is 
the beneficiary. 
6.

SUBJECT:
Parking Enforcement Services — Amendment
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve Amendment No. 4 to the agreement with Laz Parking for Citywide parking enforcement in the amount of 
$1,581,576 for a new agreement total not to exceed $9,375,151; and authorize the Chief of Police to execute the 
amendment in substantially the form presented. 
BACKGROUND:
The City of San Mateo has contracted for parking enforcement services with LAZ Parking (formerly Serco) since 2019. 
Enforcement activities in the City commenced in 2020, with a scope of work including issuing parking citations for 
observed violations in the Downtown commercial district, Residential Parking Permit Program (RPPP) zones, and for street 
sweeping violations; completing data entry and uploading citation data for processing; monitoring equipment and signage 
and reporting any damage; and performing other duties as needed such as traffic control and operational support at 
special events. The current contract was originally executed in 2021 in the amount of $2,034,004 for a term ending on 
November 30, 2022. The contract has been amended three times before. In 2022, Amendment No. 1 was executed to 
extend the term and increase compensation by $2,773,216. In 2024, Amendment No. 2 was executed to extend the term 
and increase compensation by $1,464,506. In 2025, Amendment No. 3 was executed to extend the term and increase 
compensation by $1,521,849.  
The term of the current amended contract concludes on November 30, 2026, with an option to extend for an additional 
one (1) year term. The service provided by LAZ continues to meet expectations, and staff wishes to continue to partner 
with LAZ for parking enforcement services and exercise the option to extend the contract for an additional year. 
Amendment No. 4 extends the term for one year, and increases compensation by four percent, which is aligned with prior 
annual increases.
BUDGET IMPACT:
Parking enforcement activities are funded by the Downtown Services Fund. Costs for contracted enforcement services are 
included in the Police Department’s operating budget, and the cost of Amendment No. 4 can be accommodated within the 
adopted 2026-27 budget.
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it can be seen with certainty that it will not cause a physical change in 
the environment. (Public Resources Code Section 21065.)
STRATEGIC PLAN ALIGNMENT
This staff report supports the Public Safety initiative.

SUBJECT:
Grand Jury Report: "Is Our Flock of ALPR Cameras Properly Tended?" — Response Letter
RECOMMENDATION:
Approve a letter responding to the August 3, 2026, San Mateo County Civil Grand Jury report titled "Is Our Flock of ALPR 
Cameras Properly Tended?" and authorize the Mayor to send the response of behalf of the City Council. 
BACKGROUND:
On August 3, 2026, the San Mateo County Civil Grand Jury released a report entitled "Is Our Flock of ALPR Cameras 
Properly Tended?" (Attachment 2). The purpose of the report is to examine whether Automated License Plate Reader 
(ALPR) operators in San Mateo County are in full compliance with applicable California law and whether their practices 
align with their adopted ALPR policies. The Grand Jury developed five findings and five recommendations applicable to the 
City of San Mateo. The response to each finding and recommendation requires City Council approval and is due to the 
Grand Jury no later than November 2, 2026.
The draft response letter is attached to this report as Attachment 1. In summary, staff agreed with the findings of the 
report. At the time of the Civil Grand Jury’s review, the San Mateo Police Department’s ALPR audits were not being 
completed consistently on a monthly basis. However, since the review, the Department has implemented a formal 
monthly audit process in accordance with California law, Lexipol Policy 463, and Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) 205. 
These audits provide documented oversight, reinforce accountability, and help verify that the Department’s ALPR system 
is being used appropriately and for authorized law enforcement purposes.  
Furthermore, the recommendations detailed in the report have been implemented or will be implemented. Since 
submitting its Automated License Plate Reader records, policies, and procedures to the Civil Grand Jury on December 30, 
2025, the Police Department has continued to evaluate and strengthen the ALPR program. Consistent with the 
Department’s commitment to transparency, accountability, and compliance with California law, SMPD has enhanced its 
audit process, revised its Standard Operating Procedure, and updated departmental policy. These actions reflect the Police 
Department’s continuing efforts to ensure the lawful and responsible use, oversight, and management of ALPR 
technology.
BUDGET IMPACT:
There is no budget impact associated with this action.
ENVIRONMENTAL DETERMINATION:
This action is not a project subject to CEQA, because it is an organizational or administrative activity that will not result in 
direct or indirect physical changes in the environment. (CEQA Guidelines Section 15378(b)(5).)

RESPONSE TO GRAND JURY REPORT:  “IS OUR FLOCK OF ALPR CAMERAS 
PROPERLY TENDED?” 
 
Honorable Judge Holt –  
 
Thank you for the opportunity to review and comment on the above referenced Grand Jury 
Report filed on August 3, 2026.  The City of San Mateo’s response to both the findings and the 
recommendations are listed below: 
 
Response to Grand Jury Findings: 
 
F4. The ALPR operator did not produce records demonstrating that audits required by its 
ALPR policy had been conducted.  
Response to F4:  Respondent agrees with this finding. 
 
F5. The audit reports produced by the ALPR operator were insufficient to substantiate 
compliance with applicable California law and the operator’s published ALPR policy.  
Response to F5:  Respondent agrees with this finding. 
 
F6. The ALPR operator’s Flock Safety contract does not include provisions specifically 
requiring compliance with applicable California ALPR laws.  
Response to F6:  Respondent agrees with this finding. 
 
F7. The operator’s ALPR policy described a process requiring written requests and 
executed agreements for ALPR data-sharing relationships; however, the records produced 
did not substantiate that this process was followed or that the required agreements were 
obtained.  
141 of 207

--- page 142 ---
Response to F7:  Respondent agrees with this finding. 
 
F8. The operator’s ALPR policy did not establish criteria governing the approval of in-
state ALPR data-sharing requests or the termination of existing network-sharing 
relationships. 
Response to F8:  Respondent agrees with this finding. 
 
 
Response to Grand Jury Recommendations: 
 
R4. The ALPR operator should prepare audit reports in accordance with the frequency 
stated in the operator’s published ALPR policy.  
The recommendation has been implemented. Since submitting its ALPR records to the Civil 
Grand Jury on December 30, 2025, the San Mateo Police Department has updated its ALPR 
policy and procedures and implemented monthly audits of the ALPR system. The audits are 
conducted in accordance with Lexipol Policy 463 and SOP 205. Copies of the updated policy 
and SOP are attached for reference (Exhibits A and B). 
 
R5. The audit reports produced by the ALPR operator should substantiate compliance 
with applicable California law and the operator’s published ALPR policy.  
The recommendation has been implemented.  The Department’s monthly audit process is 
designed to evaluate compliance with Senate Bill 34, Senate Bill 54, California Civil Code 
sections 1798.90.5 through 1798.90.55, Lexipol Policy 463, and SOP 205. Audit results are 
documented and reported by the Department’s ALPR System Administrator to the Chief of 
Police. The Police Department will continue to review its policies, training, audit practices, and 
oversight procedures to ensure the lawful, transparent, and accountable use of ALPR technology. 
 
R6. Upon contract renewal, the ALPR operator should incorporate contractual language 
requiring vendor compliance with applicable California law and prohibiting vendors from 
disclosing ALPR data to unauthorized third parties. Operators should consider provisions 
similar to those contained in the Town of Colma contract with Flock.  
The recommendation will be implemented.  At the time of renewal with Flock Safety or entrance 
into a new agreement with an alternate service provider, the Police Department will seek 
contractual language requiring compliance with applicable California law and prohibiting the 
disclosure of ALPR data to unauthorized third parties. The Department will review the 
contractual provisions used by the Town of Colma to determine appropriate language for 
inclusion in a renewed or new agreement. 
 
R7. The ALPR operator should update its ALPR policy to accurately reflect the process 
used to approve ALPR data-sharing relationships.  
The recommendation has been implemented. As a precautionary measure, the Police Department 
disabled all external access to SMPD ALPR data. Outside agencies may not access SMPD ALPR 
data unless the agency has entered into an approved ALPR Data Sharing Agreement and the 
sharing relationship has been reviewed and authorized in accordance with Department policy. 
The Department has updated its policy to establish a process for reviewing, approving, and 
documenting ALPR data-sharing relationships. The revised provisions require each proposed 
relationship to be evaluated for compliance with applicable law, Department policy, and a 
legitimate law enforcement purpose before access is granted. 
 
142 of 207

--- page 143 ---
R8. The ALPR operator should include in their ALPR policies written criteria governing 
the approval, denial, periodic review, and termination of in-state ALPR network-sharing 
relationships. 
The recommendation has been implemented.  The Police Department has revised its ALPR 
policy to include written criteria governing the approval, denial, periodic review, and termination 
of in-state ALPR network-sharing relationships. The revised policy establishes documented 
standards for evaluating each sharing relationship and conducting periodic reviews to confirm 
that continued access remains lawful, necessary, and consistent with Department policy.  
 
The City continues to support the ongoing work of the San Mateo Police Department to be 
leaders in the area of transparency, legitimacy and community engagement.  Since submitting its 
Automated License Plate Reader records, policies, and procedures to the Civil Grand Jury on 
December 30, 2025, the San Mateo Police Department has continued to evaluate and strengthen 
its ALPR program. Consistent with the Department’s commitment to accountability, and 
compliance with California law, SMPD has enhanced its audit process, revised its Standard 
Operating Procedure, and updated departmental policy. These actions reflect the Department’s 
continuing efforts to ensure the lawful and responsible use, oversight, and management of ALPR 
technology. 
 
This response to the Grand Jury was approved at a public meeting on October 5, 2026. 
 
 
Respectfully, 
 
 
 
Adam Lorraine

Policy
463
San Mateo Police Department
POLICIES
Automated License Plate Readers (ALPRs)
463.1   PURPOSE AND SCOPE
The purpose of this policy is to provide guidance for the capture, storage and use of digital data 
obtained through the use of Automated License Plate Reader (ALPR) technology.
463.2   ADMINISTRATION
The ALPR technology, also known as License Plate Recognition (LPR), allows for the automated 
detection of license plates. It is used by the San Mateo Police Department to convert data 
associated with vehicle license plates for official law enforcement purposes, including identifying 
stolen or wanted vehicles, stolen license plates and missing persons. It may also be used to gather 
information related to active warrants,,, suspect interdiction, and stolen property recovery.
All installation and maintenance of ALPR equipment, as well as ALPR data retention and access, 
shall be managed by the ALPR Committee Captain or their designee. The ALPR Committee 
Captain or their designee will assign members under his/her command to administer the day-to-
day operation of the ALPR equipment and data.
463.2.1   ALPR ADMINISTRATOR
The ALPR Committee Captain, as the ALPR Administrator, or their designee, shall be responsible 
for developing guidelines and procedures to comply with the requirements of Civil Code § 
1798.90.5 et seq. These guidelines and procedures are delineated in this policy and/or in our 
Department's Automated License Plate Readers Operating Procedure.
463.3   DEFINITIONS
“Automated license plate recognition operator” or “ALPR operator,” means a person that 
operates an ALPR system. For our Department purposes, this will usually refer to our personnel or 
authorized designee operating a vehicle or other item of equipment with ALPR cameras collecting 
license plate data.
“Automated license plate recognition end-user” or “ALPR end-user” means a person that 
accesses or uses an ALPR system. For our Department purposes, this will usually refer to our 
personnel or authorized designee accessing data on a retroactive basis that is collected and stored 
in a centralized database(s).
463.4   OPERATIONS
Use of an ALPR is restricted to the purposes outlined below. Department members shall not use, 
or allow others to use the equipment or database records for any unauthorized purpose (Civil 
Code § 1798.90.51; Civil Code § 1798.90.53).
(a)
An ALPR shall only be used for official law enforcement business by any Department
member(s) or authorized designee whose job duties necessitate the use of ALPR and
that have completed Department approved training.
Copyright Lexipol, LLC 2026/09/03, All Rights Reserved.
Published with permission by San Mateo Police Department
Automated License Plate Readers (ALPRs) -
488
EXHIBIT  A
144 of 207

--- page 145 ---
San Mateo Police Department
POLICIES
Automated License Plate Readers (ALPRs)
(b)
An ALPR may be used in conjunction with any routine patrol operation or criminal 
investigation. Reasonable suspicion or probable cause is not required before using 
an ALPR.
(c)
Partial license plates and unique vehicle descriptions reported during major or serial 
crimes should be entered into the ALPR system in an attempt to identify suspect 
vehicles.
(d)
No member of this department or authorized designee shall operate ALPR equipment 
or access ALPR data without first completing department-approved training. This 
training is further described in SOP 205.
(e)
A search by any Department members(s) of ALPR data shall be completed by 
providing a reason for the search, usually an agency case number, incident number, 
or a warrant number.
(f)
The employee or authorized designee using our ALPR system must recognize that 
the data collected from the ALPR device, and the content of referenced hotlists, 
consists of data that may or may not be accurate, despite ongoing efforts to maximize 
the currency and accuracy of such data.To the greatest extent possible, vehicle 
and subject information will be verified using separate law enforcement information 
sources to confirm the vehicle or subject's identity and justification for contact.Users 
of ALPR data must to the fullest extent possible visually confirm the actual plate 
characters generated by the ALPR readers correspond with the digital image of the 
license plate in question.
(g)
A search of the ALPR data may not be used to investigate, interrogate, detain, detect, 
or arrest person for immigration purposes. (CA Gov Code 7284)
463.5   DATA COLLECTION AND RETENTION
The ALPR Committee Captain or their designee is responsible for ensuring systems and 
processes are in place for the proper collection and retention of ALPR data.
All ALPR data downloaded to the server should be stored for 30 days from the collection date. 
ALPR information being used as criminal evidence will be retained until the criminal matter is 
resolved and/or in accordance with other criminal evidentiary retention schedules. All data and 
images gathered by ALPR are for the official use of the San Mateo Police Department and because 
such data may contain confidential CLETS information, it is not open to public review. ALPR 
information gathered and retained by this Department may be used and shared with prosecutors 
or others only as law permits.
Data relevant to a criminal prosecution should be exported from the ALPR system and securely 
transferred to digital evidence storage according to established department procedures.
463.6   ACCOUNTABILITY
All data will be closely safeguarded and protected by both procedural and technological means. 
The San Mateo Police Department will observe the following safeguards regarding access to and 
use of stored data (Civil Code § 1798.90.51; Civil Code § 1798.90.53):
Copyright Lexipol, LLC 2026/09/03, All Rights Reserved.
Published with permission by San Mateo Police Department
Automated License Plate Readers (ALPRs) -
489
145 of 207

--- page 146 ---
San Mateo Police Department
POLICIES
Automated License Plate Readers (ALPRs)
(a)
All ALPR data downloaded to the mobile workstation and in storage shall be accessible 
only through a login/password-protected system capable of documenting all access 
of information by name, date and time (Civil Code § 1798.90.52).
(b)
Members approved to access ALPR data under these guidelines are permitted to 
access the data for legitimate law enforcement purposes only, such as when the data 
relates to a specific criminal investigation or department-related civil or administrative 
action.
(c)
ALPR system audits should be conducted on a regular basis, as described in SOP 
205.
For security or data breaches, see the Records Release and Maintenance Policy.
463.7   POLICY
The policy of the San Mateo Police Department is to utilize ALPR technology to capture and store 
digital license plate data and images while recognizing the established privacy rights of the public.
All data and images gathered by the ALPR are for the official use of this department. Because 
such data may contain confidential information, it is not open to public review.
463.8   ALPR DATA SHARING
The Department may share ALPR data only with another California public agency and only as 
authorized by any other applicable federal or state law. The Department shall not sell, share, or 
transfer ALPR data to any private entity, nongovernmental organization, or out-of-state agency 
except as otherwise expressly authorized by law.
The following procedures are guidelines for the ALPR in-state network data-sharing relationship 
requests:
(a)
The agency makes a written request for the ALPR in-state network data-sharing 
relationship that includes:
(a)
The name of the agency.
(b)
The name of the person requesting.
(c)
The intended purpose of obtaining the data sharing-relationship
(b)
The request is reviewed by the ALPR Program Administrator and approved before the 
request is fulfilled.
(c)
The approved request is retained on file.
Requests for ALPR data by non-California public agencies will be processed as provided in the 
Records Maintenance and Release Policy (Civil Code § 1798.90.55).
APPROVAL CRITERIA
The ALPR Program Administrator shall establish and maintain written criteria for evaluating 
all requests for in-state ALPR network-sharing access. Before approving a network-sharing 
relationship, the ALPR Program Administrator shall determine that:

(a)
The requesting entity is a California public agency authorized by law to receive ALPR

(b)
The requesting agency has a publicly available ALPR Usage and Privacy Policy that 
complies with California Civil Code sections 1798.90.51 through 1798.90.55.
(c)
The requesting agency certifies that ALPR information will be accessed and used only 
for lawful purposes authorized by California law.
(d)
The requesting agency agrees to comply with all applicable security, audit, retention, 
privacy, and dissemination requirements established by law and Department policy.
(e)
A written agreement or documented authorization governing the network-sharing 
relationship has been executed and approved by the Department.
DENIAL CRITERIA
The ALPR Program Administrator shall deny a request for network-sharing access when:
(a)
The requesting entity is not an eligible California public agency.
(b)
The request is not authorized by law.
(c)
The requesting agency fails to provide the certifications or documentation required by 
this policy.
(d)
The requesting agency has demonstrated a history of misuse of ALPR information or 
noncompliance with applicable law or policy.
(e)
Approval would create an unacceptable legal, operational, security, or privacy risk.
PERIODIC REVIEW
The ALPR Program Administrator shall review each approved in-state network-sharing 
relationship at least annually to determine continued compliance with applicable law and 
Department policy.
The review shall include, at a minimum:
(a)
Verification that the participating agency remains eligible to receive ALPR information.
(b)
Confirmation that the agency continues to maintain a compliant ALPR Usage and 
Privacy Policy.
(c)
Verification that the operational need for continued sharing remains valid.
The results of the review shall be documented and retained in accordance with the Department's 
records retention schedule.
SUSPENSION OR TERMINATION
The Department may suspend or terminate a network-sharing relationship immediately whenever 
the ALPR Program Administrator determines that:
(a)
The participating agency no longer qualifies as an eligible California public agency.
(b)
The agency violates California law governing ALPR information.

463.10   ALPR DATA SEARCH AUDITS

audit is conducted of ALPR data searches at least monthly for internal ALPR users and quarterly 
for external CA public agency users. The audit shall be documented in the form of an internal 
department memorandum to the Chief of Police. The memorandum shall include any data errors 
found so any such errors can be corrected. After review by the Chief of Police, the memorandum 
and any associated documentation shall be filed and retained.

205.1   PURPOSE AND SCOPE
This Standard Operating Procedure provides guidelines for the operation and accessing of the
Department's Automated License Plate Reader (ALPR) program and data. This document is
intended to supplement Policy 463.
Our Department currently uses three independent ALPR technology platforms - Flock Safety Inc.
and AXON Fleet 3 Police Vehicle In-Car Camera System.
In accordance with the California Values Act, ALPR data may not be used for the purposes of
immigration enforcement or other similar duties.
This Standard Operating Procedure, along with ALPR Policy, shall be posted conspicuously on
our Department website.
205.2   DEFINITIONS
The following definitions for 'Operator' and 'End-User,' are listed in our Policy 463, and come from
California Civil Code 1798.
"Automated license plate recognition operator" or "ALPR operator," means a person that
operates an ALPR system. For our Department purposes, this will usually refer to our personnel or
authorized designee operating a vehicle or other item of equipment with ALPR cameras collecting
license plate data.
"Automated license plate recognition end-user" or "ALPR end-user" means a person that
accesses or uses an ALPR system. For our Department purposes, this will usually refer to our
personnel or authorized designee accessing historical data that is collected and stored in a
centralized database(s).
205.3   ALPR OPERATOR TRAINING
Prior to the operation of the our Department approved ALPR system, any person operating a
system shall complete Department approved training. The training will be coordinated by the
Department Training Division, and shall include the following:
•
A general review of the functionality of the vendor's software system
•
The authorized purposes for operating the ALPR system.
•
How to access the software system, by way of a login/password protected registry
•
How our vendor maintains logs of access and use
•
How our vendor retains and purges data from its system

Flock Safety
The Flock Safety Automated License Plate Recognition (ALPR) system consists of networked
cameras that automatically capture license plate images and associated vehicle characteristics,
including make, model, color, and other identifying attributes as vehicles pass within view of the
cameras.
The primary purpose of the system is to assist law enforcement in preventing, deterring, and
investigating crime by generating searchable ALPR data and providing real-time alerts when a
detected license plate is associated with vehicles linked to stolen vehicles, vehicles involved in
criminal activity, missing persons, AMBER Alerts, Silver Alerts, wanted individuals, or missing
persons.
All ALPR detections collected through the Flock Safety system are stored within Flock Safety's
secure cloud-based infrastructure. The system utilizes industry-standard encryption to protect
ALPR information both in transit and at rest. Administrative, technical, and physical safeguards
are implemented to protect ALPR information from unauthorized access, disclosure, modification,
or destruction, consistent with applicable security standards.
Access to ALPR information is restricted to authorized personnel for legitimate law enforcement
purposes, and system activity is subject to audit logging.
Axon Fleet 3 Police In-Vehicle Camera System
The Axon Fleet 3 Police In-Vehicle Camera System is an in-car video and digital evidence
management platform designed for public safety vehicles. The system integrates video recording,
automated license plate recognition (ALPR) functionality, and real-time connectivity into a single
platform to support law enforcement operations and evidence collection.
The system may capture ALPR detections and associated vehicle characteristics when equipped
with ALPR capabilities. This information may be used to assist law enforcement personnel
in investigating crimes, identifying vehicles associated with missing persons, criminal activity,
locating stolen vehicles, and supporting public safety operations.
Axon Fleet 3 incorporates security controls designed to protect digital evidence and ALPR
information. Data collected by the system is encrypted during transmission and while stored, and
is maintained within CJIS-compliant cloud storage environments. Access to the system and data
is restricted through role-based access controls and authentication measure

Operations Captain, or their designee, and any other person(s) as determined by the Field
Operations Captain.
